# 2025–2026 Indonesian protests

For the protests and riots involving police brutality and looting of politicians' homes, see [August 2025 Indonesian protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_2025_Indonesian_protests).

\
&#x20;Since February 2025, public and student-led anti-government demonstrations have been held throughout several cities in [Indonesia](14579.md).&#x20;

The first wave of protests were launched on 17 February 2025 by the [All-Indonesian Students' Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-Indonesian_Students%27_Union) (BEM SI),[^cite_note-detik-88] together with individual students' unions.[^cite_note-89] According to the central coordinator of BEM SI, Herianto, the alliance had called for protests all over the country on 17 and 18 February (cancelled at Jakarta), while they would hold the protest centrally at Jakarta on 19 (cancelled) and 20 February.[^cite_note-detik-88] The Civil Society Coalition had also called for civilians to participate in demonstrations on 21 February following [Friday prayers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friday_prayer).[^cite_note-90][^cite_note-91] BEM SI projected that around 5,000 students would participate in the protests,[^cite_note-92] and they also threatened further actions if the government does not react positively.[^cite_note-93]&#x20;

The second wave of protests began in March 2025 following the ratification of the newly revised [Indonesian National Armed Forces Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Armed_Forces_Law), which increased the number of civilian positions that soldiers are allowed to hold, from 10 to 14. Generally, most of the protests were held in front of the buildings of respective legislatures (national or regional), with its participants usually having worn black clothing, marked by the [burning of used tires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tire_fire) and clashes with policemen. Protests peaked in February and March 2025, but they began to fade since then.&#x20;

Starting from [Pati Regency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Pati_demonstrations), Central Java, a [third wave of protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_2025_Indonesian_protests) erupted around August 10–13, triggered by a proposed 250% increase in land and building taxes (PBB‑P2). The unrest quickly grew, drawing up to 100,000 protesters, with dozens injured.[^cite_note-94] On 25 August, thousands, including students, workers, and activists, marched to the national parliament building in Jakarta, protesting against exorbitant allowances for lawmakers.[^cite_note-95][^cite_note-96] After an [online motorcycle taxi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ojek) ([Indonesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_language): *ojek online*) driver was run over by security officers with an [armoured vehicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_armored_vehicle), protesters grew angry. It was the first recorded fatality during the six-month-long protest.[^cite_note-\:3-39] In retaliation, the demonstrators attacked two security officers who were near the location, leaving them lying on the road covered in blood.[^cite_note-97] After protesters started getting aggressive, the [military](14650.md) became involved to protect malls from looting. Protesters torched and looted the House of Representatives members' houses, including that of [Ahmad Sahroni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_Sahroni).&#x20;

## Background

The protests occurred as a response over controversial policies by Indonesian president [Prabowo Subianto](2829084.md) and his vice-president [Gibran Rakabuming Raka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gibran_Rakabuming_Raka), which include the [Free Nutritious Meals](82783358.md) (MBG) programme and the enactment of [Presidential Instruction Number 1 of 2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Instruction_Number_1_of_2025), which led to massive budget cuts, supposedly to fund it.[^cite_note-98]&#x20;

The nickname *#IndonesiaGelap* itself originates as a [hashtag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashtag_activism) popular on [Twitter](9988187.md) (X). According to *Beautynesia*, the hashtag had been used by more than 760 thousand tweets,[^cite_note-99] but within 24 hours, this number has increased up to 14 million tweets.[^cite_note-100] While according to coordinator of BEM SI, Satria Naufal, it represents "the fears, worries, and well-being of the citizens".[^cite_note-tempo-101] Digital research company Jangkara found that during February 2025, the hashtag has received 81% negative sentiment, followed by 13% neutral and 6% positive, based on their analysis on 64,816 comments on X.[^cite_note-102]&#x20;

## Timeline



"#Adili Jokowi" graffiti in Janti, [Sleman Regency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sleman_Regency), Special Region of Yogyakarta, 7 March 2025



[Presidential Instruction Number 1 of 2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Instruction_Number_1_of_2025), released on 22 January 2025

### 2025

#### February

The student protests were preceded by a similar anti-government *Adili Jokowi* ('[Try](3276812.md) [Jokowi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jokowi)') protest, which occurred at [Surakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surakarta) on 14 February 2025, demanding investigation of cases involving him. However, 10 minutes later, the protesters dispersed after heavy rain.[^cite_note-103] Alongside this, there have been instances of [graffiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graffiti) bearing the inscription *Adili Jokowi* throughout parts of Java, such as [Yogyakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yogyakarta), [Surabaya](212635.md), and [Malang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malang), much of which was later removed by local authorities.[^cite_note-104] Musician [Iwan Fals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iwan_Fals), nonetheless, has criticised this demand and questioned what are his faults in office.[^cite_note-105] Meanwhile, founder of KedaiKOPI, Hendri Satrio, has also doubted the success of this movement, stating that it would be difficult for the Prabowo's government to arrest and bring Jokowi into justice.[^cite_note-106]&#x20;

Central coordinator of BEM SI, Herianto, claimed that Indonesia "is being led by twin suns", and gave an example regarding ban on sale of 3 kg [LPG](361384.md) [gas cylinders](1179236.md) by [small informal retail traders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warung),[^cite_note-107] a policy that was enforced without knowledge of the president Prabowo Subianto and then subsequently repealed.[^cite_note-108] He also claimed that students were disappointed after Prabowo shouted "Long live Jokowi!" during the 17th anniversary of [Gerindra Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerindra_Party) at [Sentul International Convention Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sentul_International_Convention_Center). Prabowo himself has been accused by his opponents and political analysts of being Jokowi's puppet due to his closeness and the former's president influence on his policies.[^cite_note-109] Prabowo denied this accusation multiple times, the most recent being during the same 17th anniversary event of Gerindra Party, which he responded to by saying "*Ndasmu!*" ("Your head!") with a mocking tone and facial expression. Such reaction attracted criticism from the public as being childish and confrontational towards his opposition.[^cite_note-110][^cite_note-111]&#x20;

Meanwhile, during protests on 17 February, Prabowo attended a closed meeting together with his ministers of [Red and White Cabinet](77833121.md) at [Istana Merdeka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istana_Merdeka) while discussing various strategic issues such as prevention of [online gambling](2213921.md). They were also seen having lunch together.[^cite_note-112] At the same time, all eight fractions of DPR agreed to officially ratify the proposed revision of Mineral and Coal Law in 13th plenary session on 18 February.[^cite_note-113][^cite_note-114] One of the proposed change to the law was to allow universities and colleges across Indonesia to receive mining permits and become involved in mining. Proponents of this change from the government argued that this will lower cost of tertiary education, while academics and students criticized the change as potentially used to silence universities from participating in activism while worsening ecological damage caused by the mining industry.[^cite_note-115][^cite_note-116][^cite_note-117] This proposed change was later dropped when the law was passed on 17 February by the parliament the same day the protests erupted.[^cite_note-118]&#x20;

#### March

Main article: [Indonesian National Armed Forces Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Armed_Forces_Law)

In March 2025, protests began to renew after [Indonesian National Armed Forces Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Armed_Forces_Law) underwent revision by the DPR. The important points of this version, include:[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120]&#x20;

- Chapter 3:&#x20;

  - Armed Forces (TNI), previously subordinated to both the [President of Indonesia](10011631.md) and [Ministry of Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defense_\(Indonesia\)) (Kemhan), is now fully subordinated to Kemhan.

- Chapter 7:&#x20;

  - Non-war military operations (OMSP) were increased from previous 14 into 16 tasks. The newly added two tasks include protection efforts against cyberattacks, and protection of Indonesian citizens and national interests overseas.

- Chapter 47:&#x20;

  - The number of civilian positions TNI members are allowed to occupy were increased from previous 10 into 14 positions. Five of them include [National Agency for Disaster Countermeasure](7041878.md) (BNPB), [National Border Management Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Border_Management_Agency) (BNPP), [National Counter Terrorism Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Counter_Terrorism_Agency) (BNPT), [Maritime Security Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Maritime_Security_Agency) (Bakamla), and [Attorney General's Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney_General%27s_Office_of_Indonesia) (Kejagung). The proposal to add [Ministry of Marine Affairs and Fisheries](43528826.md) (KKP) into this list was shortly abandoned before its ratification.

    [^cite_note-121]

- Chapter 53:&#x20;

  - Retirement ages vary by the rank of a soldier:

  - [Non-commissioned officers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-commissioned_officer) (*bintara*) and [private](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_\(rank\)) (*tamtama*): 55 years

  - [Commissioned officers](36301328.md) (*perwira*) up to [colonel](199023.md) (*kolonel*): 58 years

  - One-star high officer (*perwira tinggi bintang 1*): 60 years

  - Two-star high officer (*perwira tinggi bintang 2*): 61 years

  - Three-star high officer (*perwira tinggi bintang 3*): 62 years

  - Four-star high officer (*perwira tinggi bintang 4*): 63 years, or can be extended twice by a presidential decree

On 19 March, Commission I of DPR and the government agreed to ratify the law next day,[^cite_note-122] and it officially took effect on 20 March during a plenary session.[^cite_note-123][^cite_note-124] This law caused strong criticism from various parties because it would potentially revive *[dwifungsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dwifungsi)*,[^cite_note-125] leading to an online petition calling for repeal of the revision on [Change.org](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Change.org) (as of 19 March, it has been signed by more than 26,000 people).[^cite_note-126] At the same time, the perception of TNI began to deteriorate following a [police raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_raid) on [cockfighting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cockfighting#Indonesia) gamble site incident at [Way Kanan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Way_Kanan_Regency), [Lampung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lampung), which led into deaths of three policemen after being shot by two soldiers[^cite_note-127][^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129] and a [test-drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Test_drive) [car theft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Car_theft), which killed a car salesman after being shot by a [Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Navy) sailor at [North Aceh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Aceh_Regency), both happened on March 17.[^cite_note-130]&#x20;

#### April

In April 2025, protests continue the second wave with [#FreePalestine](47246228.md). The hashtag erupted over Prabowo's plan to evacuate a thousand Palestinians from Gaza to Indonesia.[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132] The plan drew the attention of the [Majelis Ulama Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Ulema_Council) (MUI), so that it declared a ban on evacuating victims of the [Palestinian conflict in Gaza](74998519.md) to Indonesia because it could be interpreted as helping the interests of the [Israeli occupation](2781576.md).[^cite_note-133][^cite_note-134][^cite_note-135]&#x20;

In addition, the protests erupted over [authenticity of Jokowi's undergraduate diploma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joko_Widodo_university_diploma_controversy)[^cite_note-\:1-136] after an UGM alumnus, Rismon Hasiholan Sianipar, investigated the authenticity of the diploma.[^cite_note-137]&#x20;

#### May

The protests were accompanied by [International Workers' Day](39353050.md).[^cite_note-138]&#x20;

#### July

The country oversaw new controversial policies imposed by the government. The [Indonesian Financial Transaction Reports and Analysis Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Financial_Transaction_Reports_and_Analysis_Center) (PPATK) temporarily blocked 122 million dormant bank accounts (not being used within at least three months),[^cite_note-139] based on the evidences of abuse. However, this policy caused much condemnation leading into its reversal.[^cite_note-140]&#x20;

Alongside that, a series of anti-[transmigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transmigration_program) demonstrations were organised by [Dayak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dayak_people) indigenous communities throughout [West Kalimantan](453636.md), given its potential marginalisation of their lands, resources, and lifestyles. Previously, there were four locations in West Kalimantan that would be designated as destinations for transmigration,[^cite_note-141] namely Rasau Jaya, Gerbang Mas Perkasa, Sekayam Entikong, and Ketunggau Hulu.[^cite_note-142] Following this, the transmigration program into West Kalimantan was terminated by the government,[^cite_note-143] and such protests soon spread into [Central Kalimantan](453643.md)[^cite_note-144] and [North Kalimantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Kalimantan) in the following month.[^cite_note-145]&#x20;

#### August

Main article: [August 2025 Indonesian protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_2025_Indonesian_protests)

See also: [2025 Pati demonstrations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Pati_demonstrations)

In early August 2025, some Indonesians raised the [Straw Hat Pirates' Jolly Roger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Straw_Hat_Pirates%27_Jolly_Roger) flag from the *[One Piece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Piece)* anime franchise as a symbol of protests. This coincided with the build up to 17 August, when the country celebrated its 80th anniversary of [Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Day_\(Indonesia\)).[^cite_note-Raidi-146] This flag was first flown by truck drivers in late July 2025 to protest the administration of [Prabowo Subianto](2829084.md).[^cite_note-147] They also refused to fly any [Indonesian flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Indonesia) as a part of the demonstration against the prohibition of ODOL (over dimension, overload) trucks throughout several cities in Java, ongoing since 19 June.[^cite_note-148]&#x20;

In response, the flag was called a threat to the national unity of Indonesia by [its government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Indonesia),[^cite_note-149] especially by the leading figures from [People's Consultative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_Consultative_Assembly).[^cite_note-150] The flag has been also declared a symbol of [treason](31292.md) and waving the flag is considered as an act of [rebellion](513246.md)[^cite_note-151] and [sedition](324091.md),[^cite_note-152] while various government institutions attempted to ban this flag.[^cite_note-153][^cite_note-154]&#x20;

In August, a number of protests were held in several cities and regencies due to significant increases in [property tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Property_tax) by the local governments. Notable protests were held in [Pati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pati_Regency) (250% increase), [Bone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bone_Regency) (300%), and [Cirebon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cirebon) (150-1000%).[^cite_note-155] The protest in Pati on 13 August resulted in a clash between protesters and police, with 33 people injured.[^cite_note-156]&#x20;

#### October

On 3 October 2025, the Indonesian government suspended the license of [TikTok](56822861.md), the platform's second largest customer base, after the application refused to disclose the data of individuals who live streamed protests.[^cite_note-157] TikTok's suspension ended after it shared data with the Indonesian government.[^cite_note-158]&#x20;

### 2026

#### June

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion**. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2025%E2%80%932026_Indonesian_protests\&action=edit\&section=). *(June 2026)* |
| --- | --- |

Main article: [June 2026 Indonesian protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2026_Indonesian_protests)



Student protests in South Sumatra

On 12 June 2026, hundreds of students protest against Prabowo Subianto prompted by a [fuel](31676698.md) price hike, weakening of [Indonesian rupiah](367435.md) against other currencies such as [United States dollar](18717338.md), [Malaysian ringgit](360278.md), and [Chinese yuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_yuan), [governmental](77833121.md) tensions, and mismanaged government spending on the [Free Nutritious Meals](82783358.md) program.[^cite_note-159][^cite_note-160]&#x20;

On 15 June 2026, student protest were held across multiple cities, including [Jakarta](16275.md), [Bandung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandung), [North](453595.md) and [South Sumatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Sumatra).[^cite_note-161][^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163] The protests was happening during president of Germany [Frank-Walter Steinmeier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank-Walter_Steinmeier) visit to Indonesia.[^cite_note-164] Protesters from [Universitas Bung Karno](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Universitas_Bung_Karno\&action=edit\&redlink=1) were blockaded by the police from entering [Tugu Tani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heroes_Monument,_Jakarta),[^cite_note-165] but still managed to meet with the vice president [Gibran Rakabuming Raka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gibran_Rakabuming_Raka).[^cite_note-166] The protestors give a deadline for the government to answer by 19 June.[^cite_note-167]&#x20;

On the same day, 15 June; a group of student protesters stormed the [Gadjah Mada University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gadjah_Mada_University) hall which holds national discussion that invited 3 officials; namely [Minister of Agrarian Affairs and Spatial Planning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Agrarian_Affairs_and_Spatial_Planning) [Nusron Wahid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nusron_Wahid), Head of [Acceleration of Poverty Reduction Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acceleration_of_Poverty_Reduction_Agency) [Budiman Sudjatmiko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budiman_Sudjatmiko), and Deputy [Minister of Agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Agriculture_\(Indonesia\)) Sudaryono. Situation later become tense when group of angry student protesters start pushing security guards to get into the stage. It was also reported that the official cars were chased down and nearly vandalized by protesters after the officials were told to evacuate by the police forces.[^cite_note-168][^cite_note-169]&#x20;

On 18 June, a group of student led protestors in [Pontianak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontianak) marched onto the Digoelis Park Monument.[^cite_note-170]&#x20;

On 19 June, numerous pro-government counter protesters consisting of farmers were deployed in Jakarta mainly to support the government flagship free nutritious meal programme.[^cite_note-171]&#x20;

#### August

| \[icon] | This section **needs expansion**. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2025%E2%80%932026_Indonesian_protests\&action=edit\&section=). *(June 2026)* |
| --- | --- |

On 27 August, one year after the 2025 August riot, a mass consist of [Pati Regency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pati_Regency) citizens staged a protest in front of DPR building pressuring them to pass the asset forfeiture bill. Due to huge masses, Pati citizen representatives were let in by the lawmakers and the DPR leadership has agreed to pass the bill before 15 December as concession.[^cite_note-172]&#x20;

Shortly after the lawmakers audience with representatives of Pati citizen, a group of anarchists and vocational school students staged a riot at noon. A police station in Slipi was burned by masses at night.[^cite_note-173] The riot also subsequently disrupted the [Green Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KAI_Commuter_Rangkasbitung_Line) of commuter line.[^cite_note-174] On late night, a clashes erupt between anarchist group and pro-government organized gang, Peoples Movement for a New Indonesia (GRIB Jaya) in Slipi and Pejompongan. It was reported at least 5 trucks belonged to GRIB Jaya was destroyed by anarchist masses.[^cite_note-175] During the day of demonstration, a 59 year old local named Bambang Setiyawan died in the middle of the clash after being allegedly lynched by GRIB Jaya masses after being mistaken as protester. 322 protesters were also reportedly arrested and 18 year old schoolboy named Faturohman was also injured during the clash.[^cite_note-bbc-27-august-43]&#x20;

## Demands

### First phase: *#IndonesiaGelap* protests

A popular image supporting the student protest is based on the fictional [Emergency Alert System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_Alert_System) (EAS) shown above but with a black background, previously blue. This has led to the [bakso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bakso) P.E.N.T.O.L. movement, a [backronym](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Backronym) representing six political demands (although the following emerged on 4 February, before the protest):&#x20;

- ***P**olisi diberesin* ('The police must be fixed')

- ***E**nergi buat rakyat* ('Energy for the people')

- ***N**aikkan taraf hidup rakyat* ('Increase the people's well-being')

- ***T**unaikan tukin untuk guru, ASN dan dosen* ('Pay allowances for teachers, state employees, and lecturers')

- ***O**utput MBG diperbaiki* ('Fix the output of the free nutritious meal program')

- ***L**awan mafia tanah dan lengserkan pejabat tolol* ('Fight these goddamn land mafias and topple down stupid officials')

BEM UI presented these 5 demands during the student protests:[^cite_note-radarbanyuwangi-176]&#x20;

> \# Repeal the [Presidential Instruction Number 1 of 2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Instruction_Number_1_of_2025), being perceived that it could harm the people.&#x20;
>
> 1. Calling for status transparency of development and performance of the free nutritional meal programme.
>
> 2. Reject the attempts to revise Mineral and Coal Law and *[Dwifungsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dwifungsi)* Law.
>
> 3. Arrest and bring former president [Joko Widodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joko_Widodo) into justice.
>
> 4. Ratify the Assets Confiscation Bill.

On 17 February 2025, during the protests in [Jakarta](#Jakarta), coordinator Bagas Wisnu read out the following 13 demands to the government.[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-detik2-178]&#x20;

> \# Free, scientific, and democratic education.&#x20;
>
> 1. Genuine agrarian reforms.
>
> 2. Reject the revision attempts to the Mineral and Coal Law.
>
> 3. Remove *[dwifungsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dwifungsi)* of [ABRI](14650.md)
>
> 4. Enact the Indigenous People Bill.
>
> 5. Repeal the Presidential Instruction Number 1 of 2025.
>
> 6. Total evaluation of the free nutritious meal programme.
>
> 7. Realise the budget for lecturers' allowances.
>
> 8. Asset Confiscation Government Regulation in Lieu of Law.
>
> 9. Reject the revision attempts of [TNI](14650.md), [Polri](18251498.md), and Judiciary Law.
>
> 10. Streamline and reshuffle the cabinet.
>
> 11. Reject the revision of DPR Code of Conduct.
>
> 12. Reform of the police.

On 19 February, the original five demands presented by BEM SI have been expanded into the following seven.[^cite_note-179]&#x20;

> \# Demanding the President to repeal Presidential Instruction (Inpres) Number 1 of 2025 that could harm the people.&#x20;
>
> 1. Transparency over development status.
>
> 2. Transparency throughout the free nutritious meal, so that it can be right on target and implemented correctly
>
> 3. Reject the revision of Mineral and Coal Law that only becomes a silencer by the regime for the campus and academic environment
>
> 4. Reject *dwifungsi* of TNI that it could potentially create repression and hinder democracy.
>
> 5. Arrest and bring Jokowi to justice.
>
> 6. Enact the Asset Confiscation bill to eradicate economic crimes and corruption.

Next day, the demands above were expanded again into nine.[^cite_note-voi-180]&#x20;

The song "Bayar, Bayar, Bayar" from punk music duo [Sukatani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukatani), used as a theme song for the protest, to criticise the Police's performance which is considered unprofessional and full of [bribery](260979.md), [extortion](162449.md), and [gratification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gratification) which happened inside the Police administration in Indonesia.[^cite_note-181]&#x20;

### Second phase: *#TolakRUUTNI* protests

A second wave of demonstrations against the Draft Law on the Indonesian National Army (RUU TNI) took place across multiple regions, including Yogyakarta and Jakarta, on 19–20 March 2025. The protests involved students, lecturers, and civil society organizations, who expressed concerns that the bill could undermine democratic principles and civilian supremacy over the military. In Yogyakarta, protesters from the [University of Gadjah Mada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Gadjah_Mada) (UGM) and the [Islamic University of Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_University_of_Indonesia) (UII) presented several key demands:[^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183]&#x20;

1. Demand that the Government and DPR cancel the revision of the TNI Law, which lacks transparency, is rushed, and disregards public opinion, as it constitutes a constitutional violation.

2. Demand that the Government and DPR uphold the constitution and remain committed to the Reform Agenda by preserving the principles of civilian supremacy and equality before the law, while firmly rejecting the dual function of the TNI/Polri (*[dwifungsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dwifungsi)*).

3. Demand that the TNI/Polri, as state institutions, undergo internal reforms and enhance their professionalism to restore public trust.

4. Urge all academics across Indonesia to take a firm stance against actions that undermine democracy, violate the constitution, and deviate from the reform agenda.

5. Encourage and support civil society efforts to safeguard the Reform Agenda by actively monitoring and holding the Government and Parliament accountable.

On 19 March 2025, students from [Trisakti University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trisakti_University) staged a demonstration at Gerbang Pancasila, within the Parliament Complex in Jakarta, opposing the proposed revision of the Indonesian National Army Law (UU TNI). They presented four main demands, expressing concerns that the revisions could weaken civilian supremacy and potentially reintroduce the military's dual function (*dwifungsi* TNI) in politics and governance.[^cite_note-184] The demands were presented to Minister of Law, [Supratman Andi Agtas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supratman_Andi_Agtas). The key demands of the protest included:[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-186]&#x20;

1. Reject the entire draft revision of the TNI Law which is considered to weaken civilian supremacy and open up opportunities for the return of the dual function of the TNI.

2. Urging the removal and termination of active TNI-Polri officers from civilian positions to prevent abuse of power and corrupt practices in the military.

3. Achieve civilian supremacy and stop the neglect of the reform agenda to ensure that the military remains in the corridor of its main task as the guardian of national defence.

4. Reject all forms of [militarisation](2075211.md) in civilian government and demand the government's commitment to safeguard democratic values and human rights.

On 7 April 2025, [Suara Papua](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Suara_Papua\&action=edit\&redlink=1) finalise demands of protesters in Indonesia. These are:[^cite_note-\:2-187]&#x20;

1. Close [Freeport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freeport_Indonesia), [BP](18998720.md) [LNG Tangguh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tangguh_gas_field) and restore the people's sovereign rights.

2. Stop military operations and withdraw organic and non-organic military from all of Papua.

3. Stop the [National Strategic Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Strategic_Project) (PSN) in [Merauke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merauke), [Fak-fak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fakfak), and [Sorong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorong)

4. Close down all illegal companies throughout [West Papua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Papua_\(province\)).

5. Revoke TNI Law number 34 of 2025, reject the Police Bill, reject the Broadcasting Bill.

6. Give the right to self-determination as a democratic solution for the Papuan people.

7. Reject the [Makan Bergizi Gratis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makan_Bergizi_Gratis) Programme ('Free Nutritious Meal'; MBG).

8. Stop the criminalisation and killing of Papuans.

9. Allow foreign journalists to cover Indonesia, especially the Land of Papua.

10. Thoroughly investigate cases of human rights violations in the Land of Papua.

11. Stop the Sorong Special Economic Zone (SEZ) development project.

12. Stop the silencing of democratic space and stop the criminalisation of Papuan pro-independence activists.

13. Pass the Indigenous Peoples Bill.

14. Stop mining exploration in [Raja Ampat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raja_Ampat_Regency)

### Third phase: *17+8 Demands*

During the [August 2025 Indonesian protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_2025_Indonesian_protests), public figures and advocates published 25 demands, split into 17 demands to be enacted in one week and 8 demands to be enacted in one year.[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189]&#x20;

Due 5 September 2025:&#x20;

1. Form an independent investigative team to investigate the incidents involving Affan Kurniawan, Umar Amirrudin, as well as all victims of violence and other human rights violations throughout the protests taking place 28–30 August in a clear and transparent manner.

2. Halt all involvements of the [TNI](14650.md) in securing civil matters, and return the TNI to the barracks

3. Free all of the protestors that are currently being held and ensure that no criminal charges are pressed upon protestors.

4. Arrest, and try in a transparent court of law all of the members and commanders that ordered acts of violence (against protestors).

5. Halt all acts of violence conducted by the police and follow current guidelines set forth on controlling masses.

6. Freeze all raises and allowances for all members of the [DPR](737841.md) and cancel all new facilities (for members of the DPR).

7. Publish transparent expenditure disclosures (salaries, allowances, houses, facilities for the DPR) proactively and continuously

8. Investigate the ownership of assets for problematic members of the DPR by the [KPK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_Eradication_Commission)

9. Push the Honorary Council of the DPR to investigate members who have misappropriated the will of the people.

10. (Political) Parties must fire or impose harsh sanctions on cadre's (entrusted members of the party) who have acted unethically and sparked the anger of the public.

11. Announce the commitment of all (political) parties to side with the public in the midst of crisis.

12. Members of the DPR must involve themselves with dialogue taking place in public spaces with university students, and members of the public to increase meaningful participation.

13. Enact internal discipline to ensure the TNI does not take over the function of the [Polri](18251498.md)

14. (Announce a) Public commitment for the TNI to not enter civilian spaces during a democratic crisis.

15. Ensure fair wages for all employees within Indonesia (teachers, healthcare workers, labourers, [ojek drivers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ojek))

16. Take emergency measures to ensure no mass layoffs occur and protect the contracts of all labourers.

17. Open talks with labour unions to reach a solution for wages and outsourcing.

Due 31 August 2025:&#x20;

1. (Conduct a) Widescale cleanup and reformation of the DPR

2. (Conduct a) Widescale reform on all political parties and strengthen executive oversight

3. Assemble plans for reformation on taxation that is fairer (to all).

4. Ratify and enact the Asset Confiscation bill.

5. The strengthening and independence of the KPK, and the strengthening of the Criminal Punishments Against Corruptors Law.

6. (Conduct) Reform on the police (to ensure) professional and humanistic conduct.

7. [(Ensure the) TNI to return to the barracks, without exception](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Armed_Forces_Law#2025_revision_of_Indonesian_National_Armed_Forces_Law).

8. Reassess policies regarding the economic and workforce sector.

### Sumatra

In [Banda Aceh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banda_Aceh), students from [Syiah Kuala University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syiah_Kuala_University) (USK) organised a protest in front of the Aceh Regional House of Representatives (DPRA). They additionally demanded transparency of the use of a special autonomy fund. The head of [NasDem Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NasDem_Party)'s fraction and member of the DPRA third commission, Nurchalis, promised that the students' demands would be discussed at the legislature and carried to the national level.[^cite_note-190] The protesters left significant rubbish, ranging from used drinking bottles to banners. Since the protesters ignored the requests by journalists to clean the rubbish, it was cleaned by the police.[^cite_note-191]&#x20;



Student protestors from the [State Islamic University of North Sumatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Islamic_University_of_North_Sumatera) converse with Palacheta Subies Subianto, a representative from North Sumatra DPRD who is also a member of the [Golkar](408234.md) party in front of the building.

[^cite_note-192]

In [Medan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medan), students from the [State Islamic University of North Sumatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Islamic_University_of_North_Sumatra) protested front of the building of the North Sumatra Regional House of Representatives (DPRD) on 20 February. They carried several banners, and the building was guarded by dozens of personnel. After hours, DPRD representatives met them and guaranteed that their demands will be brought to the DPRD and national DPR.[^cite_note-193] Protests resumed next day, not long after, a representative of Commission C of the legislature, Palacheta Subies Subianto met them. Several posters were attached to the fences of the DPRD,[^cite_note-194] and used tires were burned during the event.[^cite_note-195] On 26 March, students from the North Sumatra Students' Union Alliance began to organise a sit-in front of the DPRD building at 15\:00 [WIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia), while some others were inside the building's hall after successfully jumping over its fence. The protests escalated after they burned used tires and clashed with policemen.[^cite_note-196] Next day, Accumulation of People's and Labour's Anger (AKBAR) held a protest in the same building, but later clashed with the police. According to the [Commission for Missing Persons and Victims of Violence](64465516.md) (KontraS), dozens of people were injured during the event.[^cite_note-197]&#x20;

In [Pematangsiantar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pematangsiantar), on 27 March, students from the [University of Simalungun](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=University_of_Simalungun\&action=edit\&redlink=1) clashed with policemen at Pematangsiantar DPRD, after attempting to forcefully enter the building. Three protesters were arrested by the police,[^cite_note-kompas6-69] meanwhile, one of the students, Afriadi Lesmana, was beaten up by a DPRD member and municipal policemen.[^cite_note-detik5-54]&#x20;

In [Padang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Padang), students from [Andalas University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andalas_University) (UNAND) and [Padang State Polytechnics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Padang_State_Polytechnics) (PNP) protested front of the UNAND entrance gate.[^cite_note-198] Later, hundreds of students under the All-West Sumatra Alliance of Students' Unions protested in front of the building of the West Sumatra DPRD on 18 February 2025 at 15\:00 WIB, and also held several banners.[^cite_note-ayoindonesia-199][^cite_note-200]&#x20;

In [Bukittinggi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bukittinggi), a number of community elements and the Bukittinggi student alliances, including the [Indonesian Muslim Student Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Indonesian_Muslim_Student_Union\&action=edit\&redlink=1) (SEMMI) of Bukittinggi City, held a demonstration at the Bukittinggi DPRD building on 20 March 2025.[^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202]&#x20;

In [Pekanbaru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pekanbaru), students from the Islamic University of Riau protested front of the Riau DPRD building on 26 February. They additionally demanded for the reimbursement of scholarships for 6,000 students. The protesters dispersed after submitting their demands to the parliament members.[^cite_note-203] On 14 August, protesters from University of Riau broke down the gate of governor office at Cut Nyak Dien Road, in an attempt to meet Abdul Wahid, the governor of Riau. They demanded him to answer local issues, such as wildfires, ambiguities on scholarships, and infrastructural issues.[^cite_note-204]&#x20;

In [Tanjungpinang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanjungpinang), 62 students protested front of the Riau Islands DPRD building. They were guarded by 150 joint personnel from regional and municipal police.[^cite_note-hariankepri-30]&#x20;

In [Palembang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palembang), on 20 February, hundreds of students, mainly from [Raden Fatah State Islamic University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raden_Fatah_State_Islamic_University) (UIN Raden Fatah), walked from Campus A at 10\:00 WIB and arrived in front of the building of South Sumatra DPRD at 10\:30 WIB.[^cite_note-205] They attempted to bring used tires top of a command car, but they were obstructed by forces. After negotiations, they were allowed to do so. However, after failing to meet the speaker of South Sumatra DPRD, Andi Dinialdie, protesters brought down the tires and burned them in front of the building.[^cite_note-206] It was previously projected that it would be attended by 1,000 people.[^cite_note-tribunnews-28] The protesters also gave an ultimatum of 3 × 24 hours to the government to take position regarding the budget cuts. Then, deputy speaker Ilyas Panji and member of parliament M Nasir met the protesters and agreed to reject budget cuts and to fulfill their demands.[^cite_note-207]&#x20;

In [Pangkalpinang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pangkalpinang), students from [University of Bangka Belitung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Bangka_Belitung) together with Bangka Belitung Students' Union Alliance, protested front of the building of the Bangka Belitung DPRD. They arrived at 15\:20 WIB.[^cite_note-208]&#x20;

In [Bandar Lampung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandar_Lampung), hundreds of students from various local universities protested in front of the Lampung provincial government complex. They carried an oratory car as well as several banners, one of them has the inscription *PENDIDIKAN JADI TARUHAN, INDONESIA CEMAS, DARURAT PENDIDIKAN, ALERTA ALERTA!* (Education is at stake, Indonesia is anxious, education emergency, alert alert!).[^cite_note-209]&#x20;

In [Bengkulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengkulu_\(city\)), students mainly from Muhammadiyah Bengkulu University gathered in Simpang Lima, Ratu Sambang District. They stated that they also held protests in Padang Kuas, [Seluma Regency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seluma_Regency) while stating that the student organizations there are consolidating for a bigger protest that will be held later.[^cite_note-210]&#x20;

### Java

#### Jakarta

See also: [2025 Jakarta Fairmont Hotel occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Jakarta_Fairmont_Hotel_occupation)



Students from the [University of Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Indonesia) participating in the protest

In [Jakarta](16275.md), the protests started on 17 February at Horse Statue, [Central Jakarta](2728760.md), but then spread into South Medan Merdeka and West Medan Merdeka Roads, causing traffic jams at [Bundaran HI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bundaran_HI). Thousands[^cite_note-211] of protesters shouted *hidup mahasiswa* ('long live students') and carried various banners and posters that represent their demands, such as *#krisisiklimkrisisdemokrasi* ('a climate crisis is a democratic crisis') and *kenyang kagak, bego iya* ('not yet full, damn stupid').[^cite_note-detik-88]&#x20;

Previously, student union of the [University of Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Indonesia) (BEM UI) instructed its members to wear black clothing and yellow alma maters, which is characteristic of the university.[^cite_note-212] A total of 1,623 joint personnels were deployed for security,[^cite_note-213] and traffic at West Medan Merdeka Road towards [Merdeka Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merdeka_Palace) was closed, except for the [Transjakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transjakarta) Bus.[^cite_note-214]&#x20;



Students from [Muhammadiyah and 'Aisyiyah Universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Muhammadiyah_and_%27Aisyiyah_Universities\&action=edit\&redlink=1) (PTMA) standing in front of a police blockade

They later burned used tires and several posters, including a portrait of cabinet secretary [Teddy Indra Wijaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teddy_Indra_Wijaya),[^cite_note-215] and several of them took down their almamaters under the instruction of an orator.[^cite_note-216] At 16\:00 [WIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia), the police appealed to the protesters to disperse, but they remained and threw objects such as water bottles, rubbish, and wooden slats on policemen.[^cite_note-217][^cite_note-218] One policeman was injured during the event.[^cite_note-metrotvnews-77] Public relations coordinator for [National Developmental University "Veteran" of Jakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Universitas_Pembangunan_Nasional_Veteran_Jakarta\&action=edit\&redlink=1), Bagas Wisnu, read out 13 demands to the government, ranging from educational to political issues (see [above](#Demands)).[^cite_note-detik2-178] They finally dispersed on 20\:00 [WIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia) and janitors began to clean rubbish at the site.[^cite_note-219] Disabled influencer Badru also attended the protests and prayed for the protesters.[^cite_note-220]&#x20;

The plans for a demonstration next day were cancelled by BEM SI, in preparation for peak demonstrations on 20 February at [Istana Negara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istana_Negara_\(Jakarta\)), will be hold simultaneously with the inauguration of 481 regional heads.[^cite_note-221][^cite_note-222] A day later, BEM SI organised again a protest at Arjuna Wiwaha Horse Statue, and guarded by 775 joint personnels,[^cite_note-223] although its coordinator Herianto said that they would organise a field technical this date in order to discuss its preparation.[^cite_note-224] However, the head of public relations at [Greater Jakarta Metropolitan Regional Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Jakarta_Metropolitan_Regional_Police), Ade Ary Syam, appealed to the public to maintain order during the inauguration of regional heads by not organising protests at Istana Negara this time.[^cite_note-225] The students' union of [Udayana University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Udayana_University) also decided not to participate in this protest.[^cite_note-226] The protests were held at 14\:00 WIB (protesters would gather at 13\:00 WIB), and Herianto projected that it would be attended by 3,000 students.[^cite_note-lampusatu-26]&#x20;

Half section of the West Medan Merdeka Road was closed starting from 11\:34 [WIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia) and the police used forklifts to install concrete blockades.[^cite_note-227] The Central Jakarta police deployed 588 joint personnels from Horse Statue towards front of Istana Negara,[^cite_note-228] and barbed wires were installed at the location.[^cite_note-wartakota1-229] This time, protesters from 4 students' unions[^cite_note-voi-180] arrived at 14\:37 WIB at Horse Statue, seen wearing their almamaters. They also carried various banners and pamphlets, with inscriptions such as *Efisienshit* and *1 Presiden Berbagai Insiden* ('One President Full of Incidents'). They started with giving speeches front of the blockades.[^cite_note-230] They were also seen raising up fists on their left arms.[^cite_note-231] Nada, a 44-year-old woman, distributed free foods and drinks for protesters at the location.[^cite_note-232]&#x20;

The protesters began to burn used tires near the concrete blockades at 14\:45 WIB.[^cite_note-wartakota1-229] Then, they tore down the blockades using ropes on the instruction by the orator, while the local police requested them to not do so.[^cite_note-233] After their efforts were successful, they sang national anthem "[Indonesia Raya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia_Raya)" and "[Indonesia Pusaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia_Pusaka)".[^cite_note-234] During the event, the protesters accused a man for pickpocketing, and later submitted him to nearby police post.[^cite_note-235]&#x20;



Protesters in front of [Merdeka Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merdeka_Palace) during the [Thursday Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aksi_Kamisan), raising the theme "Dark Indonesia" in support for the student demonstration, 20 February 2025

Raden Roro Neno gave a speech to protesters, encouraging them not to be trampled by the oligarchy. She also gave support for them and prayed for their success and health.[^cite_note-236] A female student read the poem "Apa Guna" by [Widji Thukul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Widji_Thukul).[^cite_note-237] One foreign national was arrested by plainclothes (suspected to be intelligence officers) while taking images during the protests.[^cite_note-era-52] He later admitted that he was not part of any foreign media outlet.[^cite_note-238]&#x20;

The protesters finally dispersed on 18\:25 WIB after [Minister of the State Secretariat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_the_State_Secretariat) [Prasetyo Hadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prasetyo_Hadi), together with his deputy Juri Ardiantoro met them and agreed to their demands. Prasetyo also requested the protesters to learn their demands within 48 hours before discussing it with student representatives.[^cite_note-239] He was seen inviting the protesters to sing "Darah Juang" together,[^cite_note-240] and hugged the protesters.[^cite_note-241] Later, Prasetyo claimed that his struggles in the government, were in line with the students' demands.[^cite_note-242]&#x20;



Protesters showcasing pro-[intelligentsia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intelligentsia) flyers

On 21 February, hundreds of civilian elements, including students without their almamaters, gathered at Ismail Marzuki Park[^cite_note-243] before protesting at Horse Statue.[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-245] They carried various banners and posters, one of them featured Prabowo's old post on Twitter from 2016.[^cite_note-246] The protesters also played the song "Bayar Bayar Bayar" by [Sukatani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukatani)[^cite_note-247] and "[The Internationale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Internationale)" translated into Indonesian by [Ki Hajar Dewantara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ki_Hajar_Dewantara).[^cite_note-248] Then, they threw away water bottles and firecrackers to policemen,[^cite_note-249] and damaged barbed wires.[^cite_note-250] Badru returned to the protests and read out all five principles of [Pancasila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pancasila_\(politics\)) for the protesters.[^cite_note-251] One protester was seen destroying a CCTV at 17\:55 WIB.[^cite_note-252] There were incidents of throwing [Molotov cocktails](20922.md), firecrackers, and fireworks, but also the burning of water barriers[^cite_note-253] on the Transjakarta busway, followed by *alerta* ('alert') shouts.[^cite_note-254] They also burned rubbish at the location,[^cite_note-255] and rampaged an intruder, allegedly an intelligence officer, in the middle of the protests.[^cite_note-256] The crowds finally dispersed at 20\:30 WIB. and janitors began to clean rubbish and extinguish fires.[^cite_note-257]&#x20;



Non-alma mater youth protesters gathering at Bundaran Patung Kuda, 21 February 2025

Around 2,460 joint personnels from regional police, military, and the regional government, were deployed for security during the protests.[^cite_note-tribunnews_metro-34] Haus distributed 1,500 cups of free drinks for protesters through 15 stands.[^cite_note-258] The fan club of South Korean boy band [NCT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCT_\(group\)), NCTzen, deployed an ambulance under the 'NCTzen Humanity x Bareng Warga' banner and distributed free foods for the protesters.[^cite_note-259] It was projected that 2,500 people would attend the protests.[^cite_note-260]&#x20;



Members of the [KontraS](64465516.md) activist group being escorted after [occupying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Jakarta_Fairmont_Hotel_occupation) the [Fairmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairmont_Hotels_and_Resorts) Hotel in [Senayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senayan,_Kebayoran_Baru), [Jakarta](16275.md), 15 March 2025.

[^cite_note-261]

On 15 March, the Civil Society Coalition led by [Andrie Yunus](84209237.md) stormed a representatives meeting held at the [Fairmont Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairmont_Hotels_and_Resorts) in [Senayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senayan,_Kebayoran_Baru) minutes before *[iftar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iftar)*. During the storming, representatives consist of the First Commission of the House of Representatives are discussing to pass the revision of the Armed Forces law behind closed door for 2 days. The protesters were later dispersed by the security guards.[^cite_note-mediaindonesia-262]&#x20;

On 19 March, the student mass protest would resume in Jakarta as group of students from [Trisakti University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trisakti_University) planned to hold a rally in front of the parliament building gate for 2 days.[^cite_note-263] At the middle of the protest, law minister [Supratman Andi Agtas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supratman_Andi_Agtas) along with 13th commission member Vita Ervina were blocked from entering the parliament building. Protesters initially tried to eject Agtas from the car despite being heavily guarded but after being pressured, Agtas voluntarily exited his car to meet the protesters.[^cite_note-264] The protesters also began to set up tents front of the gate into DPR.[^cite_note-265]&#x20;

On 20 March 2025, the [Indonesian Women's Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Women%27s_Alliance) joined the protest, demanding the DPR to withdraw the revision of Armed Forces Law.[^cite_note-tempo2-13] During the event, 5,021 joint personnels from police, military, and local government were deployed with reported minor clashes between law enforcers, camping protesters, and few journalists.[^cite_note-266] Speaker of the DPR, [Puan Maharani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puan_Maharani), answered to the protests and stated that the legislature is ready to give explanation regarding ambiguities on the law.[^cite_note-267] Meanwhile, a black flag charged with the national emblem of Indonesia and text *Indonesia Gelap* in the bottom, was flown [half-mast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Half-mast) at front of Gerbang Pancasila.[^cite_note-268]&#x20;

During the protest, 4 students from University of Indonesia and one from Muhammadiyah Prof. Dr. Hamka University were reportedly injured during the protest and were rushed to hospital after the police opened fire against the protesters.[^cite_note-dianpurnomo-48][^cite_note-albumfoto_-47] One motorcycle taxi driver was beaten by police after being mistaken as protester.[^cite_note-idntimes-87] Some segments of the fences front of the DPR were also broken down, after the crowd attempted to enter the building. Due to the situation on the street, ambulances were unable to drive to the area due to the mentioned circumstances, as a result some injured protesters were transported to nearby [Pelni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pelni) hospital using motorcycle.[^cite_note-269] One protester was reportedly missing after the protest and 5 people were arrested.[^cite_note-smipusat-270][^cite_note-barengwarga-62] At least 4 police officers suffered injuries after being hit by firecrackers thrown by protesters.[^cite_note-kompas3-78]&#x20;

Indonesian protesters wearing gear and umbrellas reminiscent of the [2020 Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%932020_Hong_Kong_protests) and [Thai Protests](64637075.md). The scene depicts demonstrators being provoked and dispersed with tear gas, readying umbrellas to counter police actions, 27 March

On 27 March, 1,824 joint personnel from the police, military, and local government were deployed in preparation for the protests at DPR building.[^cite_note-271] In order to avoid violent outbreak and future doxxing from happening, protesters begin to wear protective gears since 27 March inspired from [Hong Kong Umbrella Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umbrella_Movement) and [2019 Hong Kong protest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_Hong_Kong_protests) that happened prior and the move was later followed nationwide.[^cite_note-272] They carried various banners and stickers, such as *Negara ini asasnya Pancasila, bukan Sapta Marga* ('The ideology of this country is Pancasila, not Sapta Marga').[^cite_note-273]&#x20;

The protesters began to blockade the toll road located near the [Parliamentary Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MPR/DPR/DPD_building),[^cite_note-274] lit fireworks, and burned used tires front of the gate. Some of them attempted to climb its fences after being upset that no representative of the DPR could met the protesters.[^cite_note-275] They also burned down a police motorcycle and overran its driver, who at the moment wore civilian clothing.[^cite_note-idntimes4-81][^cite_note-276] As a response, the police attempted to forcefully disperse protesters using water cannon at 18\:20 WIB,[^cite_note-277] and some of the crowd were pushed out into [Senayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senayan,_Kebayoran_Baru).[^cite_note-278] They finally dispersed at 18\:31 WIB, and janitors began to clean rubbish throughout Gatot Subroto Road.[^cite_note-279]&#x20;

On 28 March 2025, Indonesian Mothers' Voice held a protest at the pavement of [Sarinah Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarinah_Building).[^cite_note-280]&#x20;

A smaller protest resumed on 7 April 2025 after *Lebaran*. Numerous protesters set up camping tents in front of the parliament building.[^cite_note-281] Themed *Piknik Melawan* ('Picnic to Resist'), they were forced to move into pavement next to Gelora Road on 8 April, and their tents were forcibly dismantled by 30 officers from Municipal Police next day.[^cite_note-282] The protesters have requested for attention by governor [Pramono Anung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pramono_Anung).[^cite_note-283]&#x20;

*Piknik Melawan* was held again on 14 April. The tent was to be erected right in front of the Pancasila Gate of the House of Representatives, however, the participants were forced to move to the pavement opposite. On that night, six picnic participants, two women and four men, were put into a police car. The arrests were said to have been made without interrogation and without legal representation. Half an hour or so later, those arrested were released. They regrouped and set up their tents again.[^cite_note-284]&#x20;

On 15 April, [Bivitri Susanti](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Bivitri_Susanti\&action=edit\&redlink=1), constitutional law expert, gave a public lecture at *Piknik Melawan.* The discussion between Bivitri and dozens of camp participants began at around 15\:55 WIB.[^cite_note-285]&#x20;

On 20 April, hundreds of people took part in a rally to defend [Palestine](241405.md) in front of the [US Embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Embassy_in_Jakarta) in Gambir, Central Jakarta. There is a police force that provided a special location for the masses within 50 metres of the [US Embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Embassy_in_Jakarta).[^cite_note-286] The crowd carried various paraphernalia, such as flags, scarves and accessories with [Palestinian flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Palestine). They also carried posters supporting [Palestine](241405.md) and condemning [genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_in_Palestine).[^cite_note-287] They rallied until 22\:45 WIB.[^cite_note-288]&#x20;

On 21 April, Central Jakarta local police deployed 1,211 personnel in preparation for protests at separate locations, including US Embassy, MPR/DPR building, Polri headquarters, and Sarinah Road. It was reported that 10 protests would be held in Jakarta.[^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290]&#x20;

On 28 July 2025, 1,489 joint personnel were deployed in preparation for the *Indonesia (C)emas* ('Anxious Indonesia', in reference to [Golden Indonesia 2045 Vision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Indonesia_2045_Vision)) protest at [National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Monument_\(Indonesia\)).[^cite_note-291] Protesters dispersed on 18\:00 WIB. after their demands accepted by Vice Minister of the State Secretariat, Juri Ardiantoro.[^cite_note-292]&#x20;

On 21 August, Students Together with the People Movement (GEMARAK) alongside other civilian alliances, held a protest at front of MPR/DPR building at 14\:00 WIB. Would be deployed 1,145 unarmed joint personnel in that protest.[^cite_note-293] The protesters spread out a banner with the inscription "INDONESIA SOLDOUT" and flew the [Straw Hat Pirates' Jolly Roger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Straw_Hat_Pirates%27_Jolly_Roger) flags. One orator highlighted the issue of increasing educational costs and the ownership of state assets by businessmen.[^cite_note-294]&#x20;

On 25 August, university students, online motorcycle taxi drivers (*ojol*), as well as other civilians, originally amounted up to 80 people, began to hold a protest at the MPR/DPR building at 10\:00 WIB. The protesters were coordinated by Abdul Wahid Kaliki, Raka Abimanyu, and Ade Pratama, and they demanded for the ratification of Asset Confiscation Bill. Around 452 joint personnel were deployed to the demonstration.[^cite_note-295] However, this number was soon increased to 1,250,[^cite_note-cnn2-296] and 360 additional personnel were further deployed at few places such as the Slipi Palmerah traffic light, Tomang, and Grogol.[^cite_note-297] In response to the demonstration, access into front of MPR/DPR building was closed at 10\:30 WIB, with the last being a [Transjakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transjakarta) route at 12\:30 WIB[^cite_note-298] Some junior high school (SMA) students also participated in the protest, although the head of Jakarta Metropolitan Resort Police, Purnomo Condro called to those below the age of 18 to withdraw.[^cite_note-299] The protesters were seen flying the Straw Hat Pirates' Jolly Rogers.[^cite_note-300] They also vandalised the back gate of the MPR/DPR building, with words such as *PEJABAT KEPARAT* ('officials are bastards') and *BUBARKAN DPR* ('dissolve the DPR').[^cite_note-301] During the protest, the police also distributed mineral water to protesters.[^cite_note-302]&#x20;

Over time, the protests soon turned violent. Protesters successfully broke into the smaller gate into the MPR/DPR building,[^cite_note-detik7-303] then burnt a motorcycle and damaged the glass barrier at a visitor management system. They also threw objects such as stones and glass bottles into policemen.[^cite_note-304] Outside the Parliamentary Complex, clashes between policemen equipped with trail motorcycles and student protesters occurred at Gerbang Pemuda Road,[^cite_note-305] and the protesters attempted to destroy a [Hyundai Palisade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyundai_Palisade) by damaging its windows.[^cite_note-306] On 12\:55 WIB, the police began to repel protesters into the direction of Slipi or Semanggi by using tear gas, soon after water cannons were previously shot on them.[^cite_note-cnn2-296][^cite_note-detik7-303] As a result, some student protesters escaped to railways.[^cite_note-307] Following the riot, 15 people, consisting of 4 students and 11 anarchists, were arrested by the police,[^cite_note-kompas9-71] while two protesters were injured, one of them sustained a wound on his head from thrown stones.[^cite_note-kompas8-55] A police post at Gerbang Pemuda Road was heavily damaged and being filled with rubbish,[^cite_note-308] while some structures inside the [Gelora Bung Karno Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gelora_Bung_Karno_Stadium) complex suffered from vandalism.[^cite_note-309]&#x20;

The [Labour Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour_Party_\(Indonesia,_2021\)) and the Confederation of Indonesian Labour Unions (KSPI) did not participate in the 25 August protest, and instead announced plans for a protest three days later at the same location. The chairman of KSPI, Mohammad Jumhur Hidayat, prohibited its members to participate in the former protest after finding the fact that the person responsible behind it was unclear.[^cite_note-310] The protest was also joined by university students. Initially peaceful, the protest was later turned into a riot after a molotov cocktails, fireworks, and other sharp objects were thrown at the police and the MPR/DPR building. As a result, police sprayed water cannon and subdued the protesters away from the MPR/DPR building to nearby [Plaza Senayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_Senayan) and [Senayan City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senayan_City) malls as well as Slipi underpass. Several mall visitors condemned the police brutality that was committed in front of the two malls, several student protesters were also taken to safety by mall security guards.[^cite_note-311]&#x20;

#### Malang



Theatrical action of crouching for cover in front of the Malang City Hall.

In [Malang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malang), protests were held on 18 February at the Monument Roundabout, precisely in front of the building of Malang DPRD. The demonstration in the Malang Raya region was initiated by a coalition of student representatives from Universitas Brawijaya and several student organizations based in Malang Raya. The initiators consisted of executive student leaders at the university and faculty levels, as well as members of faculty-based and extra-campus student organizations.[^cite_note-312]&#x20;

&#x20;Thousands of people came at 12\:00 [WIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia) and carried various banners and posters featuring demands, with inscriptions such as *Efisiensi untuk Oligarki X, Efisiensi untuk Rakyat ✓* 'No to Efficiency for the Oligarchy, Yes to Efficiency for the People', and *100 Hari Keadilan Dibunuh* 'Justice Is Killed in 100 Days'. Presidential portraits of Prabowo and Gibran were defaced with the text *efisienshit* on their eyes and a cross on their mouths. An hour later, the portraits, alongside used tires, were burned in front of the entrance gate towards DPRD.[^cite_note-313] After successfully meeting the speaker of DPRD, Amithya Ratnanggani Siraduhita, as well as several fraction leaders, the protesters finally dispersed at 16\:00 [WIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia) during heavy rains.[^cite_note-314]



Members of the Assembly from [Malang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malang) City DPRD in dialogue with demonstrators

On 23 March, protesters with black clothing returned to the Malang DPRD building at 16\:00 WIB. They carried banners, vandalised the fences, and wrote demands to repeal the revised Armed Forces Law and messages critical of the government on asphalt road.[^cite_note-315] They also played a theatrical featuring the reconstruction of an accident.[^cite_note-316] By 18\:00 WIB, the protests became increasingly violent and riots became prominent, with throwing of [Molotov cocktails](20922.md) and burning of TNI uniforms. Seven police and TNI personnel were injured in the protests.[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-tribunnews2-79] Meanwhile, a medical team were beaten down and verbally abused by the personnels, and their medical supplies and gadgets were seized. Around 8–10 people were missing, while dozen others were injured, with 6–7 of them were rushed to local hospitals.[^cite_note-cnnindonesia2-51][^cite_note-318] Next day following the riots, Malang regional police and [Indonesian National Student Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Student_Movement) branch in East Java went involved in a community service to clean up the building.[^cite_note-319]&#x20;

#### Surabaya



Masses protest in the framework of the rejection of the TNI Law in front of [Grahadi Negara Surabaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Grahadi_Negara_Surabaya\&action=edit\&redlink=1) on 24 March 2025

In [Surabaya](212635.md), the protests occurred before the building of the East Java Regional House of Representatives. The student protesters came from various local universities and numbered up to thousands.[^cite_note-320] They burned a bier with inscription *Indonesia Gelap* after their attempts to meet the speaker of DPRD failed. Then, they attempted to cross the wire fence, and the police fired a water cannon at them. The protesters escaped, and several threw objects, such as water bottles, at policemen.[^cite_note-suarasurabaya-321][^cite_note-322] Around 620 personnels were deployed for security during the protests. Head of the students' union of the [Airlangga University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airlangga_University), Aulia Thoriq Akbar, stated that five students have been reportedly arrested by the police.[^cite_note-ayoindonesia2-57] Two of them, each came from UNESA and UINSA ([Sunan Ampel State Islamic University Surabaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunan_Ampel_State_Islamic_University_Surabaya)), while head of operations Wibowo denied it.[^cite_note-ngopibareng-323] Novan Ziyan Ramadhan from the [State University of Surabaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_University_of_Surabaya) (UNESA) was injured after being stepped on and beaten up by policemen,[^cite_note-jpnn-44] as a result, the involved member has been reportedly questioned by the professional and security sector,[^cite_note-324] although he denied it.[^cite_note-325]&#x20;

Protests resumed in Surabaya on 21 February, and it was attended by both students and civilians. Two members of East Java DPRD from [Indonesian Democratic Party of Struggle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Democratic_Party_of_Struggle) (PDI-P), Fuad Bernardi and Yordan Batara Goa, were pettled with water bottles, after they met the protesters while in recess.[^cite_note-detik3-82] They later burned used tires, reading a poem titled 'Taek', and feature a theatrical at the site.[^cite_note-326]&#x20;



Protestors congregate for a speech in [Surabaya](212635.md), 24 March 2025

On 24 March 2025, hundreds of students with black clothing, arrived at [Grahadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Grahadi\&action=edit\&redlink=1), the official residence of the [governor of East Java](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_East_Java) at 14\:00 [WIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia). They carried various posters critical of the government and burned used tires on the road.[^cite_note-327] The protest turned increasingly violent when the protesters started to throw away stones, firecrackers, plastic bottles, and other objects to the guards. One of them threw away a Molotov cocktail to the gate toward Grahadi.[^cite_note-328] Around 1,100 personnel from Surabaya regional police were deployed during the event,[^cite_note-suarasurabaya2-37] and they forcefully dispersed the protesters at 18\:00 WIB with an armored car.[^cite_note-329] Following the event, 25 people were arrested by the police (2 of them were students),[^cite_note-kompas4-63] and 6 policemen suffered injuries.[^cite_note-suarasurabaya3-80] The former chairman of the Surabaya branch of [Indonesian National Student Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Student_Movement) (GMNI), Rizky Syahputra is among the injured victims, he suffered fracture on his left arm and laceration on the left leg.[^cite_note-330] The protest also caused serious damage on nearby Apsari Garden, leaving thousands of plants destroyed and three lamps being lost.[^cite_note-331]&#x20;

On 10 April, Surabaya regional police held a traffic engineering in preparation for a protest at Grahadi, planned to be held at 14\:00 WIB until 17\:00 WIB.[^cite_note-332] At the same time, dozens of participants wearing black clothing from Civil Society Coalition, began to hold a protest at Apsari Garden during rains at 14\:45 WIB.[^cite_note-333]&#x20;

#### Yogyakarta

In [Yogyakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yogyakarta), the Jogja Is Calling Alliance has called students and civilians to participate in the protests. Themed *#BersamaRakyat* ('Together with the People'), the long march started at Abu Bakar Ali parking lot (scheduled initially at 10\:00 [WIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia)), then moved down [Malioboro Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malioboro_Street).[^cite_note-334] Protesters were dressed in black to align with the viral hashtag. The action, led by students and members of the public, featured posters criticizing the economic, educational, social, and political policies of the Prabowo-Gibran administration. Slogans included: *"Free Food, Crisis Education," "RIP = Republic of Indonesia Patriarchy," "Let's Fight Against the Infidels," "Don't Let the Military Run Your Kitchen!"* and *"Reject [Oligarchy](22315.md), Style ABRI Dual Function."* The movement, branded Jogja Memanggil, further demanded the resignation of President Prabowo and Vice President Gibran and the dissolution of the Red and White Cabinet, citing incompetence and a perceived lack of alignment with public interests. Protesters also condemned Prabowo's response during [Gerindra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerindra)'s anniversary event on 15 February 2025, where he reportedly said *"Ndasmu!"* (a Javanese insult) to critics. Demonstrators viewed this remark as unacceptable for a head of state. Just over 100 days into the new administration, the group claims the government has already caused significant harm, leading them to call for its immediate resignation and structural overhaul.[^cite_note-335] The protestors would arrive at the Special Region of Yogyakarta DPRD building at 12\:15 WIB. Not long after, they burned used tires, threw red liquid on the building's gate and stuck a poster with the inscription *Pemerintah Tolol* ('The Government Is F\*ckin' Stupid').[^cite_note-336]&#x20;

At 14\:44 [WIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia), protesters continued the long march towards [Gedung Agung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gedung_Agung) and Point Zero of Yogyakarta.[^cite_note-337] They additionally demanded for the resignation of president Prabowo and his vice Gibran.[^cite_note-338] It was projected that 1,000 people would participate in the protests.[^cite_note-cnnindonesia-32] At the same time, several banners critical of the government were installed at the Faculty of Social and Political Sciences building at [Gadjah Mada University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gadjah_Mada_University) (UGM).[^cite_note-339] After the demonstration was over, students worked together to clean up the rubbish along Malioboro. The police who had previously escorted the crowd also helped clean up the area. In a short time, the location was clean again, allowing tourists to continue their activities. 'We want to show that this action is also about responsibility,' said Hermawan, one of the demonstrators. The Yogyakarta Police appreciated the peaceful demonstration through its official account, expressing gratitude to the students for maintaining order during the demonstration.[^cite_note-340]&#x20;

&#x20;On 25 February, students under [Muslim Students' Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_Students%27_Association) (HMI) organised a long march at [Malioboro Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalan_Malioboro) towards Special Region of Yogyakarta DPRD and then the Point Zero of Yogyakarta. They also burned street cones.[^cite_note-341]



Statue of [Raden Soedirman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sudirman) at the Yogyakarta DPRD. The graffiti reads: "Keep fighting, there are too many victims already."

Amidst concern on an upcoming draft law, the impetus for the second wave of the 2025 Indonesian protests started in Yogyakarta when the Jogja Memanggil Alliance held the 'Dark Indonesia' Volume 2 demonstration on 11 March 2025,[^cite_note-phase2-1] walking from Abu Bakar Ali Parking Lot to Yogyakarta's Zero Point in the pouring rain. Dozens of students and activists demanded President Prabowo Subianto and Vice President [Gibran Rakabuming Raka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gibran_Rakabuming_Raka) resign, while carrying posters criticising the government. The demonstration themed 'Ruwat Ruweting Penguasa Durno' was based on the concept of ruwatan in Javanese culture, which means cleansing from harm. Alliance spokesperson Hidayah said the ruwatan tradition is relevant to the current situation, where people need to be free from a corrupt regime. In addition to the theatrical action, the demonstrators highlighted BPI [Danantara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danantara), the government's strategic investment agency, which is chaired by a number of Prabowo-Gibran cabinet officials such as [Rosan Roeslani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosan_Roeslani), [Erick Thohir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erick_Thohir), and [Sri Mulyani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Mulyani). They argued that these appointments violated the Law on State Ministries. Other figures such as Jokowi, [Susilo Bambang Yudhoyono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susilo_Bambang_Yudhoyono), and former British PM [Tony Blair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Blair) were also criticised for their involvement in Danantara's advisory board and supervisory board. The crowd also criticised economic policies, including the planned 12% VAT hike, the scarcity of [BBM](23195.md), budget cuts outside of defence, as well as weak oversight that opened up loopholes for corruption. The [Indonesia Corruption Watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia_Corruption_Watch) (ICW) recorded 119 cases of [BUMN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State-owned_enterprises) corruption in 2016–2021 with state losses of IDR 40 trillion. Demonstrators criticised Prabowo for being anti-criticism, especially after his 'ndasmu' statement that offended the people regarding the Free Nutritious Meal programme.[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343]&#x20;

On 18 March, students and faculty of Gadjah Mada University organised a strike to protest against the revision of Armed Forces law which will be passed on 20 March by the House of Representatives.[^cite_note-344]&#x20;

&#x20;Another demonstration took place on 20 March 2025, this time the demonstration took place at the [DPRD of Yogyakarta Special Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Yogyakarta_Special_Region_Legislative_Council\&action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-345] This demonstration was joined by the Jogja Memanggil alliance. In addition to being followed by the Jogja Memanggil Alliance, a number of elements ranging from students at various campuses in Yogyakarta to activists were also seen accompanying, including the Cik Ditiro Forum.[^cite_note-'\:0'-346] Police were deployed, including tactical vehicles, to secure the action. The spokesperson for the action, Bung Koes, argued that the TNI Bill had the potential to threaten democracy by authorising soldiers to hold civilian positions. Marsinah, from the Jogja Memanggil Alliance, said the bill risked reviving [authoritarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism) in the style of the [New Order](2864794.md). The masses also criticised the ratification process, which was considered to be closed and procedurally flawed.



Occupation of the Yogyakarta DPRD building during the demonstration against the TNI Law, 20 March 2025

Though initially peaceful, the action turned tense at around 3.45 pm, when the crowd began to graffiti the walls of the DIY DPRD with graffiti such as 'Reject the TNI Law', 'Adili Prabowo' and [anti-police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-police_sentiment) calls such as '[ACAB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACAB)' and '1312'. The crowd also burned garbage, set off [firecrackers](418188.md), lit [bonfires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonfire), and threw things down the hall. Until the evening, the crowd remained and planned to stay overnight in the courtyard of the DPRD DIY. They set up tents, built bonfires, broke their fast, played games, and continued to hold a free pulpit while giving speeches. The action was interspersed with poetry reading and singing songs to encourage the protesters.
