The masses also called for [satay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satay), [wedang ronde](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Wedang_ronde\&action=edit\&redlink=1), and beverage vendors into the DIY DPRD environment for a meal break.[^cite_note-'\:0'-346] Despite an ultimatum from the police, the Yogyakartan DPRD office were vandalised by thrown litters and graffiti and protesters threatened to stay in front of the office indefinitely.[^cite_note-347]&#x20;



Police monitoring the demonstration at the Regional House of Representatives building of the Special Region of Yogyakarta, March 20, 2025.

The police asked the demonstrators in the yard of the DIY DPRD to disperse using loudspeakers. The demonstrators asked for permission to stay until morning, but the police only gave them one hour. Yogyakarta Police Chief Senior Commissioner Aditya Surya Dharma stated, "Please discuss it, we'll give you one hour." Meanwhile, representatives of the masses hoped that the demonstration could last all night with the concept of "camping together" to voice their opinions. However, the police stuck to their initial decision, limiting the time of the demonstration to 00.00 WIB.[^cite_note-348] At around 00.40 WIB, the police deployed two Mobile Brigade (Brimob) tactical vehicles and dozens of riot police.[^cite_note-'\:0'-346] Chaos ensued, with bottles and firecrackers being thrown at the police. Water cannons were used to push back the demonstrators.[^cite_note-349] Meanwhile, outside the DIY DPRD complex, from the south side there was another group of people trying to approach the demonstrators rejecting the TNI Law. This unidentified group in civilian clothes uttered various insults at the demonstrators rejecting the TNI Law who they considered to be the cause of the riot. A number of police personnel were seen blocking the group of residents so that they would not clash with the protesters who remained in front of the DPRD building.[^cite_note-'\:0'-346] The police prevented a clash between the two groups. The Secretary of the DIY DPRD, Yudi Ismono, said he could not yet detail the total losses due to vandalism. The DIY DPRD building, which is a cultural heritage site, experienced extensive spray-painted graffiti, as well as an incident of burning garbage. Police remained on site to prevent further damage.[^cite_note-'\:0'-346]&#x20;

On 29 March, Indonesian Mothers' Voice held a protest at [Titik Nol Kilometer Yogyakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Titik_Nol_Kilometer_Yogyakarta\&action=edit\&redlink=1) on [Malioboro Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalan_Malioboro)[^cite_note-350]&#x20;

On 15 April, hundreds of people who are members of the Ulama and Activist Defence Team (TPUA) came to the Faculty of Forestry, [Universitas Gadjah Mada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gadjah_Mada_University) (UGM), [Sleman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sleman_Regency), Yogyakarta to ask for clarification on the alleged fake diploma of the 7th President of Indonesia, [Joko Widodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joko_Widodo). They gathered in front of Room 109 of the Faculty of Forestry UGM around 07\:45 a.m, including mothers. Not long after, four representatives of the protesters entered Room 109 of the Faculty of Forestry UGM to attend an audience with the rector regarding Jokowi's diploma. Meanwhile, [Amien Rais](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amien_Rais) was also among the crowd. He said that UGM's explanation of Jokowi's previous diploma did not prove enough and instead seemed to be spinning.[^cite_note-\:1-136][^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352]&#x20;

#### Surakarta

In [Surakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surakarta), students started walking backward[^cite_note-kompas1-353] from Makutho Monument at 14\:00 [WIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia), and arrived front of the Surakarta DPRD building at Adi Sucipto Road at 15\:40 WIB, on 19 February. The protesters also carried various anti-government banners and burned used tires. Their action was guarded by hundreds of joint personnel from the military, police, and the Department of Transportation.[^cite_note-354] Coordinator Syaiful interpreted that a backward walk represents that "the nation is experiencing a setback".[^cite_note-kompas1-353] They threatened if their six demands were not realised in three days, they would return with a larger number of protesters.[^cite_note-355]&#x20;

On 19 March, hundreds of [Sebelas Maret University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebelas_Maret_University) (UNS) students held a protest rally at the Surakarta City Council. The students unfurled white banners with various writings, such as ‘Return TNI to Barracks’, as well as the hashtags *#hapuskanRUUTNI*, *#TolakRUUTNI*, *#Pukulmundur*, and *#Supremasisipil*.[^cite_note-356] The President of BEM UNS 2025 said that his side hoped that DPRD Surakarta could convey their aspirations to the central government not to pass the TNI Law. He said that the TNI Law could bring back [New Order](2864794.md) Volume 2.[^cite_note-357]&#x20;

On 20 March, students and the public participated in the demonstration against the ratification of the TNI Law wearing all black clothes. The demonstration carried the theme *Solo Raya Menggugat Indonesia Gelap* *Jilid 2* and made a long march from Al Muhlihun Mosque Surakarta to the Surakarta DPRD Building, which is about 300 metres away. The action coordinator said the demonstration at the Surakarta DPRD building was carried out as a form of disappointment over the passing of the revised TNI Law by the House of Representatives.[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359]&#x20;

On 16 April, protesters from the [Ulama and Activist Defence Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ulama_and_Activist_Defence_Team\&action=edit\&redlink=1) (TPUA) who wanted to see the authenticity of the diploma of the 7th President, [Joko Widodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joko_Widodo), came to his residence.[^cite_note-360] The atmosphere was heated around Jokowi's residence when the TPUA camp met with dozens of Jokowi's supporters who had been waiting since morning and then subsided after security officers, both the police on guard and the *[Paspampres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Security_Force_of_Indonesia)*, allowed representatives to meet with Jokowi. There were at least four representatives who met Jokowi inside his residence, one of whom was TPUA Deputy, [Chair Rizal Fadillah](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chair_Rizal_Fadillah\&action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-361] He confirmed this when met by reporters after the meeting with Jokowi. He said the purpose of their visit to Jokowi's residence was not only to stay in touch in the atmosphere of Eid al-Fitr like other residents, but also to connect with Jokowi's diploma. The meeting lasted about 30 minutes. However, Jokowi did not show his diploma to them.[^cite_note-362]&#x20;

Prior to the meeting with [Ulama and Activist Defence Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ulama_and_Activist_Defence_Team\&action=edit\&redlink=1) TPUA, Joko Widodo invited dozens of media crews including [CNN Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNN_Indonesia) to enter his residence. However, Jokowi did not allow media crews to take pictures during the meeting and was asked to collect mobile phones and cameras. He showed his diplomas from elementary to high school, and also his undergraduate degree at UGM. The UGM diploma shown by Jokowi is very similar to the image circulating on social media. However, Jokowi was reluctant to confirm the similarity of the circulating diploma photo with his own.[^cite_note-363]&#x20;

#### Other protests in Java

In [Serang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serang), students organised a sit-in at the Ciceri intersection on 20 March 2025.[^cite_note-364]&#x20;

In [Bogor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bogor), students illegally held a protest on 27 March. They blocked a road; attacked policemen with stones, firecrackers and [Molotov cocktails](20922.md); and burned water barriers. As a result, several stalls and Kujang Monument were damaged,[^cite_note-365] and 13 protesters were arrested by the police. Their actions were also condemned as 'anarchist'.[^cite_note-detik4-66] On 21 August, a group of students clashed against the [Municipal Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_Police_\(Indonesia\)) at Bogor City Hall soon after burning an effigy, causing the protesters to vandalise the building. They originally demanded Bogor Regional Public Hospital to pay off its debts and increase their service, while also questioning the responsibility behind the death of a heavy machine operator at the Galuga landfill.[^cite_note-366] Following this incident, the Cultural Heritage Expert Team (TACB) of Bogor reported the vandalisers to the police.[^cite_note-367]&#x20;

In [Bekasi](1915107.md), on 25 March, a protest was held at front of the Bekasi DPRD building. However, it caused damages on the building and 2 police cars were vandalised.[^cite_note-368] As a result, 8 protesters were arrested by the police (2 of them were already graduated and the rest were students).[^cite_note-rri3-64]&#x20;

In [Karawang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karawang), a protest that was held on 25 March ended up with clashes between protesters and authorities along with pro-government paramilitary groups. The regional DPRD building was damaged during the protest. During the protest it was widely reported that police officers were caught hijacking the ambulance carrying the wounded protesters to de re-routed to the police station instead of hospital, arresting 5 wounded protesters and 2 paramedics.[^cite_note-369] According to Cakra Legal Aid Institute, 15 people were missing during the event (and possibly also arrested by the police).[^cite_note-pojoksatu-74]&#x20;

In [Sukabumi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukabumi), on 20 March, students and civilians began to protest front of Sukabumi DPRD building at 15\:00 [WIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia). They managed to penetrate through installed barbed wires, but also burning used tires and involving in clashes with policemen. The protest was also attended by Yana Fajar, an academician from the Muhammadiyah University of Sukabumi.[^cite_note-370] During the event, a journalist from [Metro TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metro_TV_\(Indonesian_TV_network\)), Apit Haeruman, was intimidated and strangled by several student protesters.[^cite_note-netralnews-371][^cite_note-372] As a result, one protester suffered nasal fracture, and the investigation of assaults during Sukabumi protest is currently underway.[^cite_note-radarjabar-53]&#x20;

In [Bandung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandung), on 17 February, the students protested front of the building of West Java DPRD.[^cite_note-373] One participant, Ainul Mardhyah, from National Students' Front, expressed that her affirmation scholarship was being cut 10%. A daughter of migrant workers in [Malaysia](3607937.md), she also concerned about the protection of Indonesian migrant workers.[^cite_note-374] Protests later resumed on 21 February, with participants shouting *revolusi* 'revolution' and burning used tires. They also attempted to penetrate barbed wires.[^cite_note-375] As of 24 March 25 people are confirmed to be wounded after clashes with police forces and pro-government paramilitary group.[^cite_note-bdgbergerak-49] The building of [Hana Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hana_Bank) office was also burned down by the crowd,[^cite_note-376] and an investigation by the local police is currently underway.[^cite_note-377] Faqih Rohman Syafei, a journalist from [Kompas.com](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kompas.com), was beaten down by the protesting crowd after being accused as an undercover agent of State Intelligence Agency.[^cite_note-kompas2-378] The protest was resumed at Gedung Merdeka by Papua Students' Alliance on 7 April 2025.[^cite_note-379]&#x20;

In [Tasikmalaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tasikmalaya), on 19 February, more than 100 student protesters attempted to enter the building of Tasikmalaya DPRD to voice their aspirations. However, security forces carried out the entrance gate. During heavy rains, jostlings occurred until the gate collapsed, then, a water cannon started to fire on protesters. They began to throw away various hard objects into police cars, causing the glass on the security post to shatter. Regional police chief Moh. Faruk Rozi invited them into dialogue, and finally agreed to enter the building.[^cite_note-kapol-31] Three policemen and one student were injured during the event.[^cite_note-priangan-45]&#x20;

In [Indramayu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indramayu), students under the alliance of students' unions of Indramayu protested at the building of Indramayu DPRD. They attempted to smash the entrance doors of the building, causing jostlings with policemen. They also burned used tires and threw away objects to officers, due to being annoyed that the speaker of Indramayu DPRD could not meet them. Few moments later, they were able to meet the speaker and representatives of DPRD members.[^cite_note-380]&#x20;

In [Cirebon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cirebon), protests occurred front of the building of Cirebon DPRD. They were later able to enter the building, after penetrating barbed wires and police guards. Then, they challenged the deputy speaker of Cirebon DPRD, Fitrah Malik, and head of Commission I, Agung Supirno, to debate with the protesters. The protesters threatened them to resign from their positions, if unable to answer their questions.[^cite_note-381] On 24 March, dozens of students under Cirebon Is Suing Alliance arrived to protest at the same building at 14\:00 WIB, postponed from the original schedule at 09\:00 WIB. The protesters clashed with policemen, before later being allowed for dialogues with several parliament members. However, the protests intensified when they attempted to lower the national flag on the hall. It was followed by firecracker and Molotov cocktail attacks, and vandalism.[^cite_note-382]&#x20;

In [Brebes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brebes_Regency), on 28 March, students held a protest at front of the Brebes DPRD building. They were later invited by deputy speaker Iqbal Tanjung for a discussion.[^cite_note-383]&#x20;

In [Purwokerto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purwokerto), students under Community Union in Motion (Semarak) organised a long march from Prof. KH. Saifuddin Zuhri State Islamic University towards the Banyumas Regent Office. They carried several banners, among them have inscriptions such as *Sebuah Kekecewaan Republik Indonesia 1 Presiden Berbagai Insiden* ('A Disappointment of the Republic of Indonesia, One President Full of Incidents') and *Bikin Kebijakan Buat Oligarki Terus Ndasmu* ('Continue to Create Policies for the Oligarchy, Your Head!'). They also repeatedly chanted "Ganyang [Fufufafa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fufufafa)" ('Crush Fufufafa').[^cite_note-384] Protests resumed on 21 March, with students carrying a banner with inscription *Anti Rezim Fasis Prabs* 'Against Prabowo's Fascist Regime', and spread cow dung on soldiers guarding Military District Commando 0701.[^cite_note-385]&#x20;

In [Semarang](1134311.md), students under the Alliance of Students' Unions of Greater Semarang organised a protest front of the Semarang City Hall on 18 February. Thousands of students arrived at 14\:30 WIB,[^cite_note-386] and organised a long march from Pemuda Road into front of the building of Central Java DPRD. They also carried banners critical of the government,[^cite_note-387] and chanted "Ganyang Fufufafa".[^cite_note-388] After attempts to meet the DPRD members failed, and a representative came instead, the protesters mocked to enter again into the building, and threw away cow dung on the gate as a sense of their annoyance.[^cite_note-389] Field coordinator, Evan Surya from [Diponegoro University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diponegoro_University), rated the initial performance of Prabowo's government −100 from 100.[^cite_note-390] Previously, it was planned at 9\:00 WIB and projected to be attended by 1,600 people.[^cite_note-beritajateng-29][^cite_note-ayoindonesia-199] As another nationwide protest against the Armed Forces law being held across the country on 20 March, police threw tear gas and arrested 5 people, 3 of them students,[^cite_note-391] in front of the governor office and DPRD building in Semarang.[^cite_note-tempo3-60][^cite_note-kumparan1-392] One student from the [Soegijapranata Catholic University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soegijapranata_Catholic_University), was beaten down and arrested by the police, but was later released at 21\:00 WIB and rushed to the Kariadi Hospital.[^cite_note-tempo6-50]&#x20;

In [Kudus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kudus,_Indonesia), around 70 students under [Muslim Students' Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_Students%27_Association_\(Indonesia\)) (HMI), from Kudus, [Rembang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rembang_Regency) and [Jepara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jepara_Regency), organised a protest front of the building of Kudus DPRD. They also carried banners and other attributes.[^cite_note-393]&#x20;

In [Rembang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rembang_Regency), [Indonesian National Student Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Student_Movement) (GMNI) and [Muslim Students' Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_Students%27_Association_\(Indonesia\)) (HMI) held a protest in front of Rembang DPRD, 8 April 2025. They initially attempted to infiltrate the building after no member could meet them, but their actions were halted after being found by DPRD secretariat. Meanwhile, parents of Hamim Muarif, the chairman of GMNI Rembang branch, were reportedly intimidated by TNI members. This prompted the participants to move the protest to Military District Commando 0720/Rembang.[^cite_note-394]&#x20;

In [Tuban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuban), on 26 March, students from Cipayung Plus alliance protested front of the Tuban DPRD building. They also burned used tires, and carried a *[pocong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pocong)* featuring the face of Indonesian president, later to be given to several figures of the legislature, including its speaker.[^cite_note-395]&#x20;

In [Bojonegoro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bojonegoro_Regency), hundreds of black-clothed protesters from Veteran Is Calling Alliance held a protest front of the Bojonegoro DPRD building. They additionally gave speech and vandalised the building. The protesters attempted to enter Bojonegoro DPRD at 17\:00 WIB, leading into clashes with guarding policemen. The police finally dispersed the crowd with a water cannon.[^cite_note-396] As a result, dozens of protesters were injured due to being trampled, meanwhile, 4 others were arrested.[^cite_note-kabarbaik-65]&#x20;

In [Kediri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kediri_\(city\)), on 17 February, around 60 students from [Brawijaya University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brawijaya_University) organised a sit-in front of the Kediri DPRD building. They arrived using motorcycles at 14\:15 [WIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia) and carried several banners and posters rejecting the streamlining policy under the [Presidential Instruction Number 1 of 2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Instruction_Number_1_of_2025). After an hour, they met Deputy Speaker Sudjono Teguh Widjaja to discuss with the protesters.[^cite_note-radarkediri-27] On 27 March 2025, protesters from Sekartaji Alliance, consisting of various student and labour elements, held a protest at Kediri DPRD, wearing black clothing and white ribbon on their left hands. By evening, the protest went chaotic for unclear reasons, and 2 protesters were injured. Also, 26 protesters were arrested by the police, but later released after receiving assistance by Al-Amin Legal Aid Institute.[^cite_note-kompas7-68] The violence during the protest triggered condemnation by KontraS.[^cite_note-397]&#x20;

In [Blitar Regency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blitar_Regency), on 24 March, students clashed with policemen at the Blitar DPRD building, after failed to meet the parliament members.[^cite_note-398]&#x20;

In [Jombang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jombang_Regency), on 29 March, 200 students held a long march at Jombang branch of [Indonesian Journalists Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Journalists_Association), and arrived at 16\:00 [WIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia) duringrains to hold a protest front of Jombang DPRD. The protest was marked by burning of used tires and black clothing, but they also carried various banners. During the protesters were met with two parliament members. Coordinator Muhammad Hidayatulluh announced that they would hold a meeting with the speaker of DPRD next day.[^cite_note-399][^cite_note-400]&#x20;

In [Lamongan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamongan_Regency), on 17 February, students under Joko Tingkir Lamongan Students' Union organised protests at Lamongan regional government and Lamongan DPRD buildings. They were seen carrying flags and banners with inscriptions *Indonesia Gelap* ('Dark Indonesia') and *Lamongan Memanggil* ('Lamongan Is Calling').[^cite_note-401] On 27 March, students protested at Lamongan DPRD, they were seen sticking posters, drawing on the roads, and burning used tires. The protest went chaotic at night, when the protesters clashed with policemen at KH. Ahmad Dahlan Road. They escaped into coffee shops and home yards to avoid the police chase, but later pushed into Lamongan Town Square intersection by the police.[^cite_note-402] Following the event, 39 protesters were arrested, but immediately released to their families.[^cite_note-viva-67]&#x20;

In [Bondowoso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bondowoso_Regency), hundreds of students under [Indonesian Muslim Students' Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Indonesian_Muslim_Students%27_Movement\&action=edit\&redlink=1) (PMII) protested front of the building of Bondowoso DPRD. They also carried banners and posters, burned used tires, and read the [tahlil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahlil).[^cite_note-403]&#x20;

In [Cirebon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cirebon) and [Pekalongan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pekalongan), on 29 August, protestors burnt several parliamentary buildings. According to *[Detik.com](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detik.com)*, the Cirebon parliament's office equipment was looted by protestors whilst police dispersed protestors in Pekalongan.[^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405]&#x20;

### Nusa Tenggara Islands

In [Denpasar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denpasar), students under Bali Is Not Silent Alliance arrived at the building of Bali Regional House of Representatives on 11\:30 [WITA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia), and carried a banner with inscription *Tut Wuri Efisiensi*, a play on Indonesian education motto *Tut Wuri Handayani*. Ten minutes later, they were invited into the building's *[wantilan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wantilan)* (pavilion) by acting secretary I Gusti Ngurah Wiryanata to voice their demands.[^cite_note-406] Students from [Udayana University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Udayana_University) have announced plans for further protests on 31 March, following the emergence of an agreement letter between [Indonesian Army](1711324.md) and the university, which was perceived as a military attempt to intervene the education sector.[^cite_note-407]&#x20;

In [Mataram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mataram_\(city\)), students' union of the [University of Mataram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Mataram) (BEM UNRAM), stated that they would participate in protest on 20 February with president of the university's student union already departed to Jakarta to coordinate the protest with the one led by BEM SI.[^cite_note-408] They carried a bier as a form of protest.[^cite_note-409] On 25 March, hundreds of students under West Nusa Tenggara Students and Society Alliance, protested front of the West Nusa Tenggara DPRD building. Speaker of the legislature, Baiq Isvie Rupaeda, subsequently announced that their demands would be delivered to the central DPR.[^cite_note-410]&#x20;

In [Kupang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kupang), students gathered in front off East Nusa Tenggara governor office, also known as [Sasando Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sasando_Building) to protest the budget cuts for education.[^cite_note-411] On 24 March, students from Cipayung Plus Alliance protested front of the East Nusa Tenggara DPRD building.[^cite_note-412]&#x20;

### Kalimantan

| All dates in [Central Kalimantan](453643.md) and [West Kalimantan](453636.md) are in West Indonesia Time (WIB; [UTC+07\:00](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UTC%2B07\:00)); and all dates in [East Kalimantan](453651.md), [North Kalimantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Kalimantan), and [South Kalimantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Kalimantan) are in Central Indonesia Time (WITA; [UTC+08\:00](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UTC%2B08\:00)). |
| --- |

In [Pontianak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontianak), on February, dozens of students arrived at West Kalimantan DPRD for protesting.[^cite_note-413] On 27 March, several students from Panca Bhakti University protested at West Kalimantan DPRD, but their demands were later responded by several parliament members.[^cite_note-414]&#x20;

In [Palangka Raya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palangka_Raya), students from various local universities, mainly [University of Palangka Raya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Palangka_Raya) (UPR), Muhammadiyah Palangka Raya University, State Islamic Institute of Palangka Raya, and State Hindu Institute of Palangka Raya, protested at the building of Central Kalimantan DPRD. They danced with [Prabowo's campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prabowo_Subianto_2024_presidential_campaign) song *Oke Gas 2* by Richard Jersey, as a form of political satire, while also burning used tires. Some protesters also shouted out *Adili Jokowi* ('Bring Jokowi into Justice').[^cite_note-415] The students threatened the speaker of the Central Kalimantan province's parliament (DPRD) a second wave of protest in Monday with bigger mass if their demands are not met.[^cite_note-416]&#x20;



[Ratu Samban University](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ratu_Samban_University\&action=edit\&redlink=1) (UNRAS) Students hassle with the police after attempting to plaster anti-government banners on the [South Kalimantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Kalimantan) DPRD, 18 February 2025

In [Banjarmasin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banjarmasin), hundreds of students, as well as the civil society, protested in front of the building of South Kalimantan DPRD. They criticised high public approval rates of the government by various survey results, and called them not in accordance with the realities.[^cite_note-417]&#x20;

In [Kotabaru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kotabaru_Regency), on 30 March, dozens of people held a protest front of the headquarters of Military District Commando 1004, in support of the Armed Forces Law.[^cite_note-418]&#x20;

In [Balikpapan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balikpapan), students under the Students' Alliance of the Oil City started a long march from four-way intersection at Balikpapan Plaza towards Balikpapan DPRD. After arriving at the building, it was marked by jostlings between protesters and policemen at the entrance door.[^cite_note-419] They additionally criticised policies by the city's local government.[^cite_note-420] The police dispersed them after exceeding the limit at 18\:00, and later arrested 6 protesters, suspected of being provocateurs and against the officers.[^cite_note-kaltimpost-59]&#x20;



Rocky Gerung in 2020

In [Samarinda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samarinda), thousands of students protested in front of the East Kalimantan DPRD building. Political commentator [Rocky Gerung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Gerung) gave a speech to motivate the protesters.[^cite_note-421] The protesters clashed with the local police after attempting to enter the building, and they fired a water cannon on the protesters. As a result, the protesters were treated back to [Samarinda Islamic Center Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samarinda_Islamic_Center_Mosque).[^cite_note-422][^cite_note-423] On 21 March, hundreds of students from various organisations protested front of East Kalimantan DPRD. They scattered flowers as a symbolism for dying democracy, and carried a banner in opposition to the ratification of revised Armed Forces Law.[^cite_note-424]&#x20;

In [Tarakan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarakan), on 21 March 2025, Civil Supremacy Alliance, consisting of 11 students' institutions, held a protest in front of Tarakan DPRD during rains. It was also marked with burning of used tires.[^cite_note-425] On 26 March, the alliance announced for further demonstrations, after DPRD did not respond to the ultimatum issued by them previous day. Previously, they held a consolidation meeting attended by 7 representatives from 5 students' organisations.[^cite_note-426]&#x20;

### Sulawesi

#### Makassar

In [Makassar](389464.md), the following protests (except the protests in August) share very similar patterns, including burning used tires and blocking roads with a truck, effectively causing long traffic jams and creating tensions between protesters and drivers.[^cite_note-427]&#x20;

On 17 February, students protested at Perintis Kemerdekaan Road, effectively blocking the Trans-Sulawesi Road. They also burned used tires and held back a truck as a stage for protesters.[^cite_note-428] A day later, students under AMPERA conducted protests at AP Pettarani Road, Rappocini. They also burned used tires and blocked the road with a container truck.[^cite_note-429] Next day, students under Student Activists' Movement (GAM) also blocked the same road (AP Pettarani Road) with a truck and burned used tires.[^cite_note-430] Another protest occurred at Sultan Alauddin Road, attended by students from [Alauddin Islamic State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alauddin_Islamic_State_University) and carried various banners and pamphlets with critical messages. They also burned used tires.[^cite_note-431]&#x20;

Next day, Makassar Students' Alliance organised a protest under a flyover and then moved towards South Sulawesi DPRD building at Urip Sumoharjo Road. They also held two trucks as stages for the protests.[^cite_note-432] Later, the protesters burned used tires, sang together "[Buruh Tani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buruh_Tani)" by Marjinal, and pulling the barbed wires.[^cite_note-433] The protesters began to disperse at 19\:05 [WITA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia), and police chief of Makassar, Arya Perdana, claimed that their aspirations have arrived into South Sulawesi DPRD.[^cite_note-434] However, as of 19\:40 [WITA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia), students from the [State University of Makassar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_University_of_Makassar) (UNM) and a group of drivers, were seen fighting each other using stones and bows.[^cite_note-435] As a result, 6 people, accused of being provocateurs, were arrested by the local police while hiding within the campus of UNM,[^cite_note-cnnindonesia1-58] although they were later sent home by 04\:00 [WITA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia)[^cite_note-436]&#x20;

On 24 February, protesters from 16 organisations, including student elements, arrived below the flyover of Urip Sumoharjo Road at 14\:45 [WITA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia) They also wore ropes on their left hands and prepared other attributes, including banners and a loudspeaker.[^cite_note-437] Protests started at 3\:35 [WITA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia), and used tires were burned during the event. They began to disperse at 17\:00 WITA.[^cite_note-438]&#x20;

On 19 March, protesters under Muslim Students' Association (HMI) protested front of South Sulawesi DPRD at Urip Sumoharjo Road. They additionally burned used tires and gave a speech at middle of the road. The protesters finally began to disperse on 17\:41 WITA after being accepted by parliament members.[^cite_note-439]&#x20;

On 5 August, students from the State University of Makassar held a protest under a flyover road at AP Pettarani Road. They were seen flying Jolly Roger flags (see [#Background](#Background)) and carrying posters containing their demands, deeming that the proposed revisions to the [Criminal Procedure Law of Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Criminal_Procedure_Law_of_Indonesia\&action=edit\&redlink=1) (KUHAP) are not transparent and involve little public participation.[^cite_note-440] One motorcycle driver was forced to get off his motorcycle and almost being assaulted by protesters after attempting to infiltrate them. Another protest occurred at South Sulawesi DPRD building, and they finally dispersed on 16\:30 WITA.[^cite_note-441]&#x20;

On 25 August, several alliances, including Makassar Democratic People's Federation and Contesting People's Union, as well as students from the Students' Union of State University of Makassar (UNM) and the Faculty of Law at the Islamic University of Indonesia, held protests at various places in Makassar. Among the locations include the buildings and headquarters of Makassar DPRD, South Sulawesi DPRD, Bank Sulselbar, Pertamina, RS Cahaya Medika, and the Alauddin-Pettarani Junction. They highlighted several corruption cases, which involved the construction project of a library in [Pangkajene Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pangkajene_Islands_Regency), the main ideas of [Bone Regency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bone_Regency) funds, and the corporate social responsibility funds from [Bank Indonesia](5044785.md) (BI) and [Financial Services Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_Services_Authority_\(Indonesia\)) (OJK)[^cite_note-442]&#x20;

#### Other cities in Sulawesi

In [Palopo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palopo), non-governmental organisation Aspirasi held a protest front of Palopo mayor office on 29 March in support of Armed Forces Law.[^cite_note-443]&#x20;

In [Kendari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kendari), students from [Haluoleo University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haluoleo_University) protested front of the building of Southeast Sulawesi DPRD, and 100 joint personnels were deployed to guard the protests. They rejected budget cuts in various sectors.[^cite_note-444]&#x20;



A still image of a student successfully overpowering a policeman in a duel atop a police truck in [North Sulawesi](453688.md) became a rallying cry for continued protest, 20 March.

[^cite_note-445]

[^cite_note-446]

In [Manado](844858.md), the student protesters under North Sulawesi Movement Alliance organised a [sit-in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sit-in). They blocked the 17 Agustus Road, precisely front of [Samsat](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Samsat_\(Indonesia\)\&action=edit\&redlink=1) Office, effectively causing traffic jams. Hundreds of personnels were deployed by local police at the North Sulawesi Governor's Office, then the protesters attempted to break the entrance gate, tearing apart several pieces of iron. They also burned a bier and used tires.[^cite_note-447] After waiting for three hours, they were finally permitted to enter the governor's office by Head of National and Political Unity Body of North Sulawesi, Johny Suak.[^cite_note-448] Later on 20 March, students under North Sulawesi Forward Alliance protested at North Sulawesi DPRD building. After being unable to meet parliament members, they infiltrated barricades installed by the police and clashed with them, causing damage within the building. Clashes between protesters and policemen began at 05\:30 p.m., and three protesters were arrested during the event.[^cite_note-idntimes3-61]&#x20;

In [Palu](1432484.md), hundreds of students gathered in front of the Central Sulawesi DPRD, mainly from [Tadulako University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tadulako_University). The protest almost turned violent with students trying to get through the building gate.[^cite_note-449] One student was injured in the head as the result of the commotion.[^cite_note-kailipost-46]&#x20;

In [Mamuju](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mamuju_\(city\)), [West Sulawesi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Sulawesi), protests also occurred in Simpang Lima road held by dozens of students who demanded the free nutritious meal program to be evaluated.[^cite_note-450]&#x20;

In [Bone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bone_Regency), a demonstration rejecting the increase in the Rural and Urban Land and Building Tax (PBB-P2) rates ended in chaos. The demonstration prompted the Bone Regency Government to temporarily decide to postpone the 65% increase in PBB-P2 rates. The demonstration, escorted by 1,000 joint Indonesian army and police personnel, took place at the Bone Regent's office on 19 August. The demonstration, organized by the Bone People's Alliance (*Aliansi Rakyat Bone*), initially proceeded peacefully during the day. However, it then turned anarchic, the demonstrators threw firecrackers and molotov cocktails, which were responded to by security officers by throwing tear gas, while carrying out sweeps and forced dispersal.[^cite_note-451][^cite_note-452]&#x20;

### Maluku Islands

| All dates are in [Eastern Indonesia Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Indonesia_Time) (WIT; [UTC+09\:00](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UTC%2B09\:00)). |
| --- |

In [Ambon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambon,_Maluku), students from the State Islamic Institute of Ambon (IAIN Ambon), with their almamaters, protested in front of the Maluku DPRD building.[^cite_note-453] The students rejected the free meal program and demanded the Presidential Instruction Number 1 of 2025 repealed while stating what Indonesia needs is free education.[^cite_note-454][^cite_note-455]&#x20;

In [Ternate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ternate), hundreds of students gathered in front off the Ternate mayor office building and protested the free meal program by the government. The students laid out several demands such as free education, to repeal the Presidential Instruction Number 1 of 2025, allowances for teachers and university lecturers, as well as local issues such as demanding 50% reserved employment quota for women in the city government, rejecting land reclamation program in the city, and concern about the city's piped water infrastructure.[^cite_note-456] The protest later turned violent with two journalists reportedly assaulted by the city's [Municipal Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_Police_\(Indonesia\)).[^cite_note-Satpol-20] The next day on 25 February 2025, journalists and media workers in the city held a separate demonstration condemning the assault. [Alliance of Independent Journalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_of_Independent_Journalists) and Indonesian Union of Journalist's North Maluku chapter also condemned the incident as against freedom of press and a breach of laws.[^cite_note-457]&#x20;

In [Morotai Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morotai_Island_Regency), protest was held predominantly by Pasifik University (UNIPAS) in front off the regent's office and parliament building (DPRD). The students brought a banner written "*Republik oligarki, Indonesia gelap, Malut butuh pendidikan gratis*" (Oligarchic republic, dark Indonesia, North Maluku needs free education). The students criticized the free meal programs is ineffective and what Indonesian students need is free education.[^cite_note-458]&#x20;

### Papua

| All dates are in Eastern Indonesia Time (WIT; [UTC+09\:00](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UTC%2B09\:00)). |
| --- |

In [Manokwari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manokwari), on 10 April, students' union of the [University of Papua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Papua) (UNIPA) held demonstrations at three separate places: the UNIPA campus, Mansinam hostel, and Makalo stop signal. The participants were guarded by Manokwari regional police, and they also carried several banners, such as *UU TNI mempercepat genosida dan ekosida di Papua* ('Armed Forces Law hastens genocide and ecocide in Papua'). During the event, two protesters were arrested by the police. The crowd negotiated with West Papua DPRD, [West Papua People's Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=West_Papua_People%27s_Council\&action=edit\&redlink=1), and the police.[^cite_note-jagatpapua-70]&#x20;

In [Wamena](13012812.md), thousands of students held a demonstration as a form of rejection of the government's free nutritious meal program on Monday, 17 February 2025. The students rejected the program, stating that what they need is instead free education and improvement on facilities. It is estimated that around 3,500 students participated, consisting of middle school, high school and university students. Thousands of students came from [Wamena](13012812.md), [Jayawijaya Regency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jayawijaya_Regency) and [Yahukimo Regency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahukimo_Regency), based on the police information.[^cite_note-tempo4-7] A riot occurred at around 09.00 [WIT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Indonesia_Time), when the crowd moved towards the Menara Salib located in front of the Jayawijaya Regent's office and took place on Hom-Hom Road, several hundred meters from the Jayawijaya regent's office.[^cite_note-459]&#x20;

In [Paniai](22831495.md), thousands of students from all levels (elementary, middle school, high School and vocational school) held a demonstration against the free nutritious meal (MBG) program on Monday, 24 February 2025. They went to the Paniai Regent's Office in Madi Village, [East Paniai District](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=East_Paniai_District\&action=edit\&redlink=1) and asked the government to prioritize free education.[^cite_note-redaksikoran-8][^cite_note-460]&#x20;

In [Deiyai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deiyai_Regency), a Free Nutritious Meal (MBG) protest was held and attended by thousands of students from dozens of schools. They carried out a long towards the Deiyai regent's office. Wearing elementary, middle and high school uniforms, the students walked from the [Thomas Adii Football Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Adii_Football_Field\&action=edit\&redlink=1) at around 09.30 [WIT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Indonesia_Time) to the regent's office.[^cite_note-461]&#x20;

In [Jayapura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jayapura), a protest action by students of the [Cenderawasih University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cenderawasih_University) was carried out on Wednesday, 26 February 2025. They delivered their aspirations directly to the Papuan [House of Representatives](737841.md) regarding the Law on the Indonesian National Army and demanded the repeal of the law.[^cite_note-462] On 7 April, students who are members of the central leadership committee of the Independent Forum of West Papua Students (FIM-WP) conducted a demonstration against [Freeport Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freeport_Indonesia) at Ekspo Waena and Perumnas III Waena, Heram District. This peaceful protest started from the Cenderawasih University campus and moved towards the Perumnas III taxi round. However, the protest was forcibly dispersed by joint TNI-Polri officers.[^cite_note-\:2-187]&#x20;

In [Nabire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nabire), peaceful demonstration held by protesters who reject [PT Freeport Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freeport_Indonesia). The protesters organised a long march to the office of the Central Papua Provincial [House of Representatives](737841.md). Unfortunately, the police forbade it because it would disturb public order. The demonstration lasted until it was forcibly dispersed.[^cite_note-\:2-187]&#x20;

### Overseas

The earlier overseas protest were organised by group of Indonesian students studying at [Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University) in [New York City](645042.md) under the name of *Jong Columbia* on 21 February from 16\:00 [WIB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Indonesia) until 18\:00 WIB in New Residence Hall, [Teachers College, Columbia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teachers_College,_Columbia_University) which gathered more than 100 students. At the same time the leaders of Indonesian Students Union in Japan, Malaysia, and The Netherlands published an open letter expressing their solidarity with the student union protesters back home.[^cite_note-463] The [Indonesian diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_diaspora) in Germany planned solidarity protests at the [Brandenburg Gate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandenburg_Gate), [Berlin](3354.md), on 1 March.[^cite_note-464] The Indonesian diaspora in Australia organised solidarity protests at the [State Library Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Library_Victoria), [Melbourne](17306237.md), on the same day.[^cite_note-465] Indonesian students in Columbia University would later return to protest against the revision of Armed Forces law in front of Teachers College.[^cite_note-logos-2]&#x20;

After the announced increase in housing allowance followed by the killing of motorcycle taxi (*ojol*) driver Affan Kurniawan, several more protests occurred in multiple countries. In the United States, more than 500 protesters gathered under the name of Amerika Bergerak (previously Jong Columbia) between 4 and 6 September in [New York City](645042.md) and several other cities.[^cite_note-466] In Germany, protesters gathered in late August and early September in [Aachen](1520.md),[^cite_note-467] [Berlin](3354.md),[^cite_note-468] [Bonn](3295.md),[^cite_note-469] Hannover,[^cite_note-470] and [Cologne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cologne).[^cite_note-471] Indonesian diaspora in the Netherlands held a demonstration on 4 September 2025, by marching from [ISS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Institute_of_Social_Studies) at the [Erasmus University Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erasmus_University_Rotterdam) to the [Indonesian Embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_Indonesia,_The_Hague) in [The Hague](30269.md). This action was intended as a form of solidarity for Affan Kurniawan, as well as all victims of state violence.[^cite_note-472]&#x20;

## Reactions

### Domestic

#### Protesters



Graffiti on the DPR walls during the 2025 Indonesian protest mimicking [*Fraternal Kiss*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/My_God,_Help_Me_to_Survive_This_Deadly_Love). Recreating this image is a clause for arrest.

[^cite_note-473]

A lecturer of the Faculty of Economy and Business at the [University of Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Indonesia) (UI), Rizki Nauli Siregar posted [the image above but with black background](#Demands), while also supporting students in her class to participate in the protests.[^cite_note-474] A lecturer of the Faculty of Social and Political Sciences at [Gadjah Mada University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gadjah_Mada_University) (UGM), Subarsono, stated that a political demonstration is a sign of living democracy.[^cite_note-475] Another lecturer of Faculty of Social and Political Sciences at UGM, Muchtar Habibi, claimed that the protests could be strengthened by widening its participation.[^cite_note-476] Professor at [Airlangga University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airlangga_University), Henri Subiakto viewed the protests not just as a spontaneous movement, but also as a form of accumulation of their disappointment of political condition in Indonesia.[^cite_note-477] He later agreed with [Mahfud MD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahfud_MD)'s statement that 'Indonesia is still dark'.[^cite_note-478] Academic and historian Andi Achdian viewed them as a shock for Prabowo's government.[^cite_note-479]&#x20;

[Rocky Gerung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Gerung) viewed that the protests were not just an expression of pessimism, but also as a reflection of the actual condition.[^cite_note-480] He also later criticized Prabowo's response to the protests as insufficient and further highlighted Prabowo's rhetorics that continuously praised Jokowi as a sign of his undue influence on him.[^cite_note-481] The fan club of South Korean boy band [NCT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCT_\(group\)), NCTzen, announced their support for the protesters, and they would provide free foods, drinks, and medical needs through crowdfunding (see [#Jakarta](#Jakarta)).[^cite_note-482] Fan club of another South Korean boy band [Super Junior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Junior) also similarly proposed such.[^cite_note-liputan6-483]&#x20;

#### Government



Police stand guard at the Medan DPRD

During an exclusive interview on 7 April 2025, president [Prabowo Subianto](2829084.md) called out the demonstrations as a 'provocation', and alleged that foreign parties (such as [USAID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Agency_for_International_Development)) funded them.[^cite_note-484] On 1 September, he also mentioned that he would confront "mafias" responsible for allegedly stirring up protests.[^cite_note-485]&#x20;

Head of the [Presidential Communication Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Communication_Office), [Hasan Nasbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasan_Nasbi), confirmed amidst the protests that funds allocated to the colleges, [Kartu Indonesia Pintar](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Kartu_Indonesia_Pintar\&action=edit\&redlink=1) for college, and scholarships will not be cut.[^cite_note-486] Minister of the State Secretariat, Prasetyo Hadi, responded that Indonesia "is not really dark" as the slogan suggests, and called for the students to be optimistic.[^cite_note-487] Vice [Minister of Higher Education, Science, and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Higher_Education,_Science,_and_Technology_\(Indonesia\)), Fauzan asked the student protesters to increase their intellectual abilities.[^cite_note-488] The spokesman for the Presidential Communication Office, Adita Erawati, confirmed that a few demands presented by the protesters have been answered by the government, such as tuition fees not being increased nor scholarships being discontinued.[^cite_note-489] Fellow spokesman Ujang Komarudin responded that Indonesia is still 'bright', not 'dark' as the slogan suggests. He also confirmed that there would be no cuts to scholarships, tuition fees would be not raised, and free health check would continue.[^cite_note-490]&#x20;

The speaker of [People's Consultative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_Consultative_Assembly) (MPR), [Ahmad Muzani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_Muzani), judged the public reaction during the protests as excessive and contraproductive.[^cite_note-491] Deputy speaker of MPR, [Eddy Soeparno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eddy_Soeparno) called for the students to be optimistic and claimed that pessimism only brings the situation into a stalemate.[^cite_note-492] The deputy speaker of [House of Representatives](737841.md) (DPR), Adies Kadir, regarded the demonstrations as a form of creativity by the students to voice their aspiration.[^cite_note-493] [Luhut Binsar Pandjaitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhut_Binsar_Pandjaitan), Chairman of the [National Economic Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Economic_Council_\(Indonesia\)) (DEN), responded to the #IndonesiaGelap hashtag, which was used by student demonstrators protesting various government policies. He argued that Indonesia has been progressing well, despite certain shortcomings, which he noted are not unique to the country but are also experienced by other nations, including the United States. Luhut rejected the notion that Indonesia is in a dire state and that the government is progressing well. He stated that the protesters are 'dark', and not Indonesia.[^cite_note-494]&#x20;

Anticipating the coordinated nationwide protests on 20 February which coincides with inauguration of regional heads elected from [2024 Indonesian local elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Indonesian_local_elections), [Indonesian National Police](18251498.md) (Polri) urged students to not stage any protests in the vicinity of the state palace or disrupt the inauguration ceremony.[^cite_note-495][^cite_note-496][^cite_note-497]&#x20;

Deputy speaker of the DPR, [Sufmi Dasco Ahmad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufmi_Dasco_Ahmad), viewed the protests against the Armed Forces Law as normal in a democratic country.[^cite_note-498] Regarding the 25 August protest at the Parliamentary Complex, the Deputy Speaker of the Commission II at DPR, Aria Bima did not found any problem with it and instead called for protests if necessary.[^cite_note-499]&#x20;

#### Censorship and repression

CCTV footage showing members of the TNI intimidating and spying on KontraS's main office at night following the revision of the TNI Law.

[^cite_note-500]

The government's reaction has also been marred by censorship, mainly targeting journalists and creative industry workers. It was widely reported that the Indonesian new wave duo Sukatani, who wrote a song criticising the police forces, was intimidated, and was later forced to make an apology video as part of the intimidation. American music critic [Anthony Fantano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Fantano) condemned the repression, describing the situation as being "not good".[^cite_note-501][^cite_note-502] [Amnesty International](18947898.md) also urged the [Indonesian National Police](18251498.md) to investigate the intimidation case.[^cite_note-503] On X, a video surfaced showing a foreign citizen journalist being apprehended by police for "not having a permit".[^cite_note-504]&#x20;

There were also incidents of [doxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doxing) of journalists following the protests at Jakarta on 21 February, causing condemnation by Legal Journalists' Union (Iwakum).[^cite_note-505] [CNN Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNN_Indonesia), which is under the umbrella of [Trans Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans_Media), was under public scrutiny for making two discrediting headlines against the protesters. In response to the headlines, two CNN Indonesia journalists were doxed by social media users sympathetic to the protesters, and several others called for a boycott of Trans Media-affiliated outlets.[^cite_note-506]&#x20;

After the storming of [Fairmont Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairmont_Hotels_and_Resorts), [Commission for Missing Persons and Victims of Violence](64465516.md) (KontraS) reported being terrorised by unknown assailants. KontraS vice coordinator Andrie Yunus stated that the unknown individuals had been frequently visiting their office and three KontraS staff members had received intimidating phonecalls.[^cite_note-507]&#x20;

Prior to the ratification of the Armed Forces law amendment, the parliament building and its surrounding areas were heavily guarded by armed forces and police forces.[^cite_note-508]&#x20;

Several media outlets in Indonesia reportedly received intimidation from the government officials. *[Kompas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kompas).com* journalist Adhyasta Dirgantara reportedly received a death threat from the adjutant of the [Commander of the Indonesian National Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander_of_the_Indonesian_National_Armed_Forces) after questioning the military's assault on the Tarakan police station.[^cite_note-509] On 19 March, Indonesian magazine *[Tempo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tempo_\(Indonesian_magazine\)),* known for its critical stance on the government, received a threat when a severed pig's head was delivered to its office in [Palmerah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmerah), Jakarta, targeting journalist Francisca Christy Rosana. Editor-in-chief Setri Yasra condemned the incident as intimidation against freedom of the press.[^cite_note-510][^cite_note-Tempo-511] The next day, a journalist from the digital media outlet [IDN Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IDN_Times) was intimidated while filming the protest outside the parliament building. Several officers attempted to seize his mobile phone and motorcycle key.[^cite_note-512] Two days later, *Tempo* received another anonymous package containing a box of six headless rats.[^cite_note-Tempo-511] As of 26 March, the Independent Journalists' Alliance had recorded that 18 journalists had become victims of violence during protests against the revision of Armed Forces Law.[^cite_note-cnnindonesia3-84] On 27 March, during the Jakarta protest, another journalist from *Kompas.com,* Rega Almutada, was intimidated and searched by individuals believed to be law enforcement in civilian clothing, while two other journalists from Russian media outlet [RT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RT_\(TV_network\)) were forced to turn off their cameras.[^cite_note-513] In response to growing concerns over threats to press freedom, Hasan Nasbi, serving as Chief of the Presidential Communication Office, reaffirmed the government's commitment to upholding media independence. However, his remarks drew criticism on social media after he reportedly stated that *Tempo* should "cook the pig's head."[^cite_note-Tempo-511]&#x20;

Additionally, there have been found incidents of intimidation against medical officers by policemen. [Jakarta Legal Aid Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_Aid_Institute_\(Jakarta\)) alleged that the perpetrators would not be punished and the repressive approach had been intentionally designed inseparably from the police.[^cite_note-514]&#x20;

In May 2025, a female student of [Bandung Institute of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandung_Institute_of_Technology) was arrested after posting an AI generated meme depicting Joko Widodo and Prabowo Subianto doing a parody of *[My God, Help Me to Survive This Deadly Love](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/My_God,_Help_Me_to_Survive_This_Deadly_Love)* painting, a kiss between [Leonid Brezhnev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonid_Brezhnev) and [Erich Honecker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erich_Honecker). The arrest has triggered widespread condemnation on X.[^cite_note-515]&#x20;

#### Others

There were calls to translate the hashtag into various foreign languages, such as English, French, Arabic, Japanese, Mandarin, as well as few regional languages such as Javanese.[^cite_note-liputan6-483] Actor and singer [Angga Yunanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angga_Yunanda) posted a tweet in support of the protests.[^cite_note-516] Through hashtag *#ArtistBersuara* ('Artists Speak Up'), digital artists on Twitter (X) also gave support for the protesters.[^cite_note-517] Meanwhile, in response to March protests, mural artists have followed suit to criticise the government.[^cite_note-518] CEO of Malaka Project, Ferry Irwandi, viewed the protests as a 'concern and love in order to make Indonesia better'.[^cite_note-519]&#x20;

Senior activist Faizal Assegaf through his Twitter account, gave full support for the protesters in 'the fight against injustice, arrogance, authoritarianism, and intimidation', and shouted *Ganyang Jokowi* ('Crush Jokowi').[^cite_note-520] He also countered [Luhut's statement](#Government) and called him not to intimidate the protesters.[^cite_note-521] He later claimed that Prabowo's presidency could become 'meaningless' without [Titiek Suharto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titiek_Suharto), and this statement was criticised by the Love Prabowo Movement.[^cite_note-jpnn1-522] Nusantara Youth Forum (FPN) called for his arrest at the headquarters of Polda Metro Jaya and Polri on 20 February.[^cite_note-523]&#x20;

The head of Love Prabowo Movement (GCP), Kurniawan stated that the student protests are a form of their aspiration, while also warning them not to be infiltrated by certain groups.[^cite_note-524] Later, he alleged that the protests were an attempt to influence public opinion and to weaken Prabowo and the state by a handful of people. He also criticised that Faizal's statements could cause instability within the country. Dony Endrassanto viewed that Faizal's statements point to a *[coup d'État](25914154.md)* attempt and treason against the government.[^cite_note-jpnn1-522] An opinion page on *KedaiPena.com* spread a claim that these protests constitute a "[colour revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colour_revolution)" that orchestrated by the [National Endowment for Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Endowment_for_Democracy) (NED).[^cite_note-525] Meanwhile, [Labour Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour_Party_\(Indonesia,_2021\)) through its vice chairman Rivaldi Haryo Seno, issued a condemnation of police brutality during protests against revised Armed Forces Law.[^cite_note-526]&#x20;

Chairman of the Executive Council of [Nahdlatul Ulama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahdlatul_Ulama), [Yahya Cholil Staquf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahya_Cholil_Staquf), questioned what are the grounds to organise the protests.[^cite_note-527] Vice secretary-general of [Golkar Party](408234.md), Sosialisman Hidayat Hasibuan, alleged that the protests are a form of political pressure on efforts by the government to arrest [Hasto Kristiyanto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasto_Kristiyanto), secretary-general of [PDI-P](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Democratic_Party_of_Struggle).[^cite_note-528] The head of media and opinion gathering at Golkar Party, [Nurul Arifin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nurul_Arifin), while suggesting that the protests could become a 'warning alert' for the government, she also reminded that ways to convey aspirations should be done constructively, not anarchistically.[^cite_note-529][^cite_note-530] [Mahfud MD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahfud_MD) claimed that there are so many 'bright' policies by the government, and need to be respected.[^cite_note-531]&#x20;

Chairman of [Indonesian Chamber of Commerce and Industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Chamber_of_Commerce_and_Industry), [Anindya Bakrie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anindya_Bakrie) viewed the protests as normal in a democratic country.[^cite_note-532] Former secretary of the [Ministry of State-Owned Enterprises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_State-Owned_Enterprises), [Said Didu](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Said_Didu\&action=edit\&redlink=1), through his Twitter account, was confused why the *#IndonesiaGelap* protests focused on toppling down Prabowo, not to bring Jokowi to justice, while according to himself, the condition was caused by Jokowi's faults.[^cite_note-533] Policy observer Gigin Praginanto, through his Twitter account, satirised that the protests' theme should be better changed from *Indonesia Gelap* to *Indonesia Gelap Banget Nget Nget* ('Indonesia Is Very, Very, Very Dark').[^cite_note-534] Actor [Jefri Nichol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jefri_Nichol) said that he regretted for participate in a protest, the last one occurred in 2023, when he wasn't able to memorise any content of the chapters while being asked by a journalist.[^cite_note-535]&#x20;

There has been call from some internet users urging people to boycott celebrities and products that are supportive toward the government. Several state-owned enterprises such as [Pertamina](4042974.md) (which had found itself in a concurrent consumer boycott over allegations of adulterated fuel that surfaced during a corruption investigation)[^cite_note-536] and three state-owned banks were also being targeted by the boycott movement. Few celebrities who are accidentally included in the list would later make clarification that they have nothing to do with the government programmes.[^cite_note-537][^cite_note-538][^cite_note-539]&#x20;

#### Businesses

After the death of *ojol* driver that caused riot around Mobile Brigade Corps headquarters in [Kwitang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kwitang), the nearby Atrium [Senen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senen) mall announced via their social media that the mall will be temporarily shut down until further notice due to safety concern.[^cite_note-540] Aside from Atrium Senen, another mall [Senayan Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senayan_Park) that was located near the MPR/DPR building announced that they only open the mall until 12\:30 WIB on 29 August 2025 as well as announcement postponement of Impactnation Japan Festival to October which was scheduled to be held on 30–31 August 2025 at Senayan Park. [Sarinah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarinah) also reduced its operating hour to 16\:00 WIB.[^cite_note-541]&#x20;

On 29 August, Several luxury brands in [Plaza Senayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_Senayan) such as [Dior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dior), [Fendi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fendi), [Louis Vuitton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Vuitton), and others was seen emptying their stores displays in order to avoid lootings by angry mobs in aftermath of the 28 August riot that happened in front of the mall.[^cite_note-542] Several luxury brands that opened their stores in [Plaza Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_Indonesia) and [Grand Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Indonesia) malls which are located at [Jalan M.H. Thamrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalan_M.H._Thamrin) were also reportedly close their stores until further notice.&#x20;

On 29 August 2025, there were image leaked on X that the [Sudirman Central Business District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sudirman_Central_Business_District) in Jakarta that hosted the [Greater Jakarta Metropolitan Regional Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Jakarta_Metropolitan_Regional_Police) headquarters has been heavily barricaded and sterilized on the second day of protest.[^cite_note-543]&#x20;

In Surabaya, Plaza Surabaya and its nearby shops were evacuated and was forced to close earlier after protesters stormed the Grahadi.[^cite_note-544] Another mall in Surabaya, [Tunjungan Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tunjungan_Plaza) also stopped its operation earlier than usual, on 19\:00 WIB due to unrest has reached [Tunjungan Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tunjungan_Street) where the mall is located.[^cite_note-545]&#x20;

### International

#### Government

On 30 April, the [United States Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Government) issued a level 2 travel warning to Indonesia citing the possible protest that could turn violent.[^cite_note-546] On 29 August, the [Embassy of the United States, Jakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_the_United_States,_Jakarta) issued alert to every American nationals in Indonesia in the wake of protest in front of the Mobile Brigade Corps headquarters in Kwitang which is located close to the U.S. embassy in Jakarta.[^cite_note-547]&#x20;

At the same time, the [Taipei Economic and Trade Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taipei_Economic_and_Trade_Office,_Jakarta,_Indonesia) in Jakarta issued a warning to all Taiwanese nationals in Indonesia to avoid protest areas.[^cite_note-548]&#x20;

The [Canadian Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Government) issued a travel warning to Indonesia in the light of the 28 August protest that turned violent.[^cite_note-549]&#x20;

The [British Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Government) updated its travel warning regarding Indonesia on 29 August warning every [British](19097669.md) nationals in Indonesia to avoid joining protests.[^cite_note-550]&#x20;

#### Others

[International non-governmental organization](4142591.md) [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) (HRW) issued a statement on 19 March condemning the passing of military law amendment for threatening human rights in Indonesia. HRW also urged the parliament to drop the proposed amendment that would enable military to fill the civilian positions in the judiciary and state-owned enterprises.[^cite_note-551]&#x20;

[Amnesty International](18947898.md) via its representative in Indonesia also criticised the passing of the military law. Amnesty International then explained the danger of the military law that have been passed by the parliament and found misconducts within its passing.[^cite_note-552] Earlier, Amnesty International secretary general [Agnès Callamard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agn%C3%A8s_Callamard) visited Indonesia to evaluate the human rights situation in Indonesia.[^cite_note-553]&#x20;

[Reporters Without Borders](526054.md) (RSF) through the director of its Asia-Pacific bureau, Cédric Alviani, has also issued condemnation of violence against journalists during Indonesian protests.[^cite_note-554] Other international NGOs such as the German and Belgian chapters of [International Federation of Action by Christians for the Abolition of Torture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Federation_of_Action_by_Christians_for_the_Abolition_of_Torture) (ACAT), All Arakan Students' & Youths's Congress, Accountability Counsel, and Advocacy Forum-Nepal also published a joint statement condemning the passing of the amendment.[^cite_note-555]&#x20;

[United Nations](31769.md) special rapporteur on the situation of human rights defenders [Mary Lawlor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Lawlor_\(human_rights_advocate\)) expressed her concern about repression against the human rights NGOs during the protest and urge Indonesian government to ensure the voice of civic organisations are guaranteed without repression.[^cite_note-556]&#x20;

The US based military newspaper, [*Stars and Stripes*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stars_and_Stripes_\(newspaper\)), published a piece on 8 September 2025, argued that demonstrations in Indonesia, while framed as public anger over lawmakers' perks and economic pressures, were being orchestrated by entrenched elites resisting President [Prabowo Subianto](2829084.md)'s anti-corruption reforms. It contends that the unrest reflects elite pushback against transparency and modernization, rather than grassroots democratic dissent.[^cite_note-557]&#x20;

## Impact

The student protests were featured as a front cover for the 18 February 2025 online edition of *Lentera* newspaper.[^cite_note-558] As of 18 February, the [rupiah](367435.md) began to weaken by 0.38% amidst the protests,[^cite_note-559] however, the currency began to strengthen by 0.10% on 21 February.[^cite_note-560] In response to the protests, there are currently attempts to counter *#IndonesiaGelap* on various social media platforms through the hashtag *#IndonesiaCerah* ('#BrightIndonesia'), originating from an [Instagram](31591547.md) account benpro.tv.[^cite_note-561]&#x20;

According to a senior researcher at Citra Institute, Efriza, predicted that then-[Minister of Higher Education, Science, and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Higher_Education,_Science,_and_Technology_\(Indonesia\)), [Satryo Brodjonegoro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satryo_Brodjonegoro) could be replaced in a reshuffle due to the current student-led protests.[^cite_note-562] This reshuffle was later confirmed and took effect on 19 February, with Brodjonegoro sacked from the position.[^cite_note-563][^cite_note-564] [Rocky Gerung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Gerung), however, demanded to the government to give the explanation about his sacking, and claimed that the students' movement has been prepared months involving various experts.[^cite_note-565]&#x20;

On 21 March, seven students from the Faculty of Law at the [University of Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Indonesia), submitted a judicial review on the revised Armed Forces Law to the [Constitutional Court of Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Indonesia). They noticed numerous irregularities during its revision, deemed as 'unconstitutional' and 'hurried'.[^cite_note-566] This move was soon followed by the Civil Society Coalition.[^cite_note-567]&#x20;

As the country situation doesn't show any sign of improvement, many young Indonesians have decided to flee the country to search for a new life as the government keep responding to the protest in bad manners.[^cite_note-568]&#x20;

### Market decline



[Indonesia Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia_Stock_Exchange) Composite Index during closing around 9\:37 AM on 18 March 2025

In March 2025, Indonesia's financial markets faced a severe jolt as the [Jakarta Composite Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jakarta_Composite_Index) (JCI) plunged by over 7% in a single trading session. This sharp [intraday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intraday) decline—recorded on 18 March—was the steepest since 2011. The plunge triggered an automatic 30-minute trading halt as markets faced a decline of 6.146% during closure at around 9\:37 AM,[^cite_note-569] a mechanism designed to stabilize markets at 5% during periods of excessive volatility. After a 30-minute halt, trading resumed but the index continued to decline, reaching a 7.1% drop at one point. The sharp decline in Indonesia's stock market quickly rippled across other sectors, with technology and manufacturing companies posting the biggest losses. Shares of [DCI Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=DCI_Indonesia\&action=edit\&redlink=1), a leading provider of data center infrastructure, plunged around 20 percent, falling to 115,800 [rupiah](367435.md) (approximately \$7). Meanwhile, [Chandra Asri Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chandra_Asri_Pacific\&action=edit\&redlink=1), a major player in energy and chemicals, saw its stock price drop nearly 19 percent by the afternoon.[^cite_note-570] During trading, foreign investors made a net sell of IDR 2.48 trillion, continuing the trend of outflows totalling IDR 29.41 trillion since the beginning of the year. Foreign ownership in the JCI also fell to 2.9%, the lowest since 2011, reflecting a decline in global interest in the Indonesian market. This contrasted with other Asian bourses that rallied.[^cite_note-\:0-571] The negative market sentiment was fuelled by concerns over national economic conditions. The sudden downturn reflected a deepening crisis of investor confidence, rooted not only in macroeconomic uncertainties but also in political anxieties and shifting fiscal policies under President Prabowo Subianto's administration.[^cite_note-572] In February alone, the state budget (APBN) recorded a deficit of IDR 31.2 trillion, while the [consumer confidence index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consumer_confidence_index) and [savings ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Average_propensity_to_save) fell, indicating weak purchasing power. Layoffs increased, with major factories such as [Yamaha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yamaha_Motor_Company) and PT [Sanken Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Sanken_Indonesia\&action=edit\&redlink=1) closing. Tax revenue also weakened, with Income Tax 21 falling 39.5% YoY and IPR declining by 0.5% signalling consumption pressure. There was also a general decline in credit disbursement in Q3 2025 across many provinces, with the slowest growth being SME loans and consumer loans.[^cite_note-573][^cite_note-574] This was attributed to lower consumer purchasing power and confidence as well as unstable political landscape.[^cite_note-575] During this period, banks began shifting to corporate and commercial loans to avoid the increased risk in consumer and SME loans, where NPLs have experienced the highest increase. This trend occurred despite existing incentives from the Central Bank, such as lowered interest rates, reflecting the [credit rationing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Credit_rationing) phenomenon observed during the early days of the reformation era.[^cite_note-576] Systemic risk looms over the capital market, given the potential impact on the financial sector.[^cite_note-\:0-571]&#x20;

Although [macroeconomic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macroeconomics) factors and controversial [fiscal policies](132645.md) of the Prabowo Subianto administration were the main culprits, public discourse and political debates also highlighted the revision of the TNI Law (RUU TNI) as a factor that worsened market sentiment.[^cite_note-577] According to a [Tempo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tempo_\(Indonesian_magazine\)) report, economist from CELIOS, Nailul Huda, stated that the decline in the JCI has been occurring since 14 March, and the TNI bill is one of the triggers. He highlighted concerns that the military's expanded role in the civilian and economic spheres could lead to legal uncertainty, erode [meritocracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meritocracy), and cause investor fear. One example is the appointment of an active military officer as President Director of [Perum Bulog](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Perum_Bulog\&action=edit\&redlink=1), which fuelled perceptions of military intervention in the business sector.[^cite_note-578][^cite_note-579] Deputy Chairman of Commission I of the House of Representatives [Budi Djiwandono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budi_Djiwandono) immediately reassured and denied that the TNI Bill was the direct cause of the JCI crash, asserting that there are no articles in the revised draft that explicitly regulate the placement of active TNI officers in state-owned enterprises.[^cite_note-580] On the other hand, economists such as [Wijayanto Samirin](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Wijayanto_Samirin\&action=edit\&redlink=1) cited the revision of the TNI Law as one of the five factors that caused the market crash, citing public concerns over the potential return of ABRI's dual function. This view reflects the market's sensitivity to the issue of militarisation, even if only as a perception.[^cite_note-581]&#x20;

The response from Indonesian authorities was swift but indicative of broader systemic stress. The [Financial Services Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_Services_Authority_\(Indonesia\)) (OJK) introduced temporary measures allowing publicly listed firms to buy back shares without requiring prior shareholder approval—a move aimed at supporting valuations and signaling confidence. Concurrently, [Bank Indonesia](5044785.md) intervened in the foreign exchange market to stabilize the rupiah, deploying a combination of rate tools and reserves. These interventions did moderate the pace of capital outflows but did little to resolve the underlying concerns surrounding fiscal direction and political stability.[^cite_note-582]&#x20;

By August, the JCI had recovered and broken its previous all-time record.[^cite_note-583]&#x20;

After the 25–28 August protest turned violent, the Rupiah weakened 0.86% to the U.S. Dollar with a value of IDR 16,455 per U.S. Dollar the following Friday morning.[^cite_note-584] The incident also caused another Indonesian stock market crash with the IDX Composite experiencing a 2% decline in the Friday morning market session, due to a high amount of panic selling among foreign investors.[^cite_note-585]&#x20;

## Investigation

On 17 June 2025, the [Attorney General's Office of Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney_General%27s_Office_of_Indonesia) (Kejagung) revealed during a press conference, a video of Marcella Santoso confessing that she had created numerous negative content surrounding Armed Forces Law, including *#IndonesiaGelap* protests and a petition calling for its repeal. A day later, she quickly denied her own confession after being interrogated by Kejagung. Marcella herself is an advocate who previously involved in acquittal during a crude palm oil (CPO) corruption scandal, money laundering, and [obstruction of justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Offences_against_public_justice), the latter of which also involving fellow advocate Junaedi Saibih and the news director of [Jak TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jak_TV), Tian Bahtiar. Director of investigation at Kejagung, Abdul Qohar admitted that the investigators from Solicitor General for Special Crimes (Jampidsus) have not yet entered that issue.[^cite_note-586]&#x20;

Head of Information Centre at TNI, Major General [Kristomei Sianturi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kristomei_Sianturi) found a number of proofs regarding the funds by Marcella flowing into [buzzers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buzzer_\(internet\)), [non-governmental organisations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-governmental_organisation) (NGOs), and other foundations, amounting up IDR 500 million and US\$2 million, while also investigating its actors and their motives.[^cite_note-587] Executive Director at Amnesty International Indonesia, [Usman Hamid](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Usman_Hamid\&action=edit\&redlink=1) criticised that the video reveal has no relevance with the ongoing investigation process involving her, while also alleging "attempts to intimidate students' and civilian movements".[^cite_note-588]&#x20;

- [2019 Indonesian protests and riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Indonesian_protests_and_riots) – another nationwide, students-led protest during Joko Widodo's presidency

- [Indonesia omnibus law protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia_omnibus_law_protests) – protests against the Job Creation Act

- [2022 Indonesian student protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Indonesian_student_protests) – protests against rumours of the delay of the 2024 elections
